- **séif 23, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וְלָכֵן אֲפִלּוּ אִם יִפְסֹק … אֲפִלּוּ קֶשֶׁר אֶחָד אַחַר הַחֻלְיָא … כֵּיוָן שֶׁלֹּא נַעֲשׂוּ עֲדַיִן כָּל הֶכְשֵׁר צִיצִית … בִּכְדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲשֶׂה שׁוּם עֲשִׂיָּה מֵהַצִּיצִית בִּפְסוּל » → ולכן אפילו אם יפסוק אחר כך ראשי החוטין אין מועיל כלום, כיוון שגוף הציצית כבר נעשו בפסול … אפילו קשר אחד אחר החוליא … כיוון שלא נעשו עדיין כל הכשר ציצית הכתובה בתורה בפסול קודם שפסק ראשי החוטין, אין לפסלן משום "תַּעֲשֶׂה וְלֹא מִן הֶעָשׂוּי כְּבָר בִּפְסוּל" … בכדי שלא יעשה שום עשייה מהציצית בפסול
- **séif 27, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וְלֹא בְּמִנְיַן הַקְּשָׁרִים שֶׁבֵּין הַחֻלְיוֹת » → of the קשרים between the חוליות
- **séif 27, FR** (portée depuis la page EN/HE (passage de reprise, demandé par l'arbitre)) — « שֶׁבֵּין הַחֻלְיוֹת » → qui sont entre les חוליות
- **séif 27, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « שֶׁבֵּין הַחֻלְיוֹת » → שבין החוליות
- **séif 28, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וְחוֹזֵר וְקוֹשֵׁר שְׁנֵי פְּעָמִים זֶה עַל גַּבֵּי זֶה » → ties twice again one over the other
- **séif 28, FR** (portée depuis la page EN/HE (passage de reprise, demandé par l'arbitre)) — « וְחוֹזֵר וְקוֹשֵׁר שְׁנֵי פְּעָמִים זֶה עַל גַּבֵּי זֶה » → on noue de nouveau deux fois l'un sur l'autre
- **séif 28, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « זֶה עַל גַּבֵּי זֶה ; כִּי הָאֶחָד לֹא יִתְקַיֵּם וְאֵינוֹ חָשׁוּב קֶשֶׁר כְּלָל » → שתיים זה על גבי זה ; שהאחד לא יתקיים ואינו חשוב קשר כלל (la seconde ajoutée dans ce passage, pour la parité avec FR et EN)
- **séif 31, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וְיַרְחִיק מְעַט וְיִכְרֹךְ עוֹד שָׁלֹשׁ פְּעָמִים » → spaces a little and winds 3 more times
- **séif 31, FR** (portée depuis la page EN/HE (passage de reprise, demandé par l'arbitre)) — « וְיַרְחִיק מְעַט » → on écarte un peu (second écart)
- **séif 31, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « יַרְחִיק מְעַט … וְיַרְחִיק מְעַט … שָׁלֹשׁ פְּעָמִים » → ירחיק מעט … וירחיק מעט … ג׳ פעמים (le premier « מעט » ajouté dans ce passage)
- **séif 34, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « אִם כֵּן כְּשֶׁיַּעֲטֹף אֶת קוֹמָתוֹ … וְלֹא יִהְיֶה תּוֹלֶה וְנוֹגֵעַ וּמַכֶּה עַל הַכָּנָף ; צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַגְּדִיל תָּלוּי » → garment's width, then when he wraps his stature from head to foot in the garment's width, the גדיל … and would not hang touching and striking the corner … the גדיל must hang
- **séif 34, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « אִם כֵּן כְּשֶׁיַּעֲטֹף אֶת קוֹמָתוֹ מֵרֹאשׁוֹ עַד רַגְלָיו בְּרֹחַב הַבֶּגֶד — יִהְיֶה הַגְּדִיל תָּלוּי כְּלַפֵּי הַקַּרְקַע, וְלֹא יִהְיֶה תּוֹלֶה וְנוֹגֵעַ וּמַכֶּה עַל הַכָּנָף ; פְּעָמִים צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַגְּדִיל תָּלוּי » → du vêtement, alors, quand on enveloppe sa stature de la tête aux pieds dans la largeur du vêtement, le גדיל pendrait vers le sol, et il ne pendrait pas en touchant et frappant le coin ; … il faut que le גדיל pende
- **séif 34, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « אִם כֵּן כְּשֶׁיַּעֲטֹף אֶת קוֹמָתוֹ … וְלֹא יִהְיֶה תּוֹלֶה וְנוֹגֵעַ וּמַכֶּה עַל הַכָּנָף ; צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַגְּדִיל תָּלוּי » → אם כן כשיעטוף את קומתו מראשו עד רגליו ברוחב הבגד, … ולא יהיה תולה ונוגע ומכה על הכנף … צריך שיהיה הגדיל תלוי

### Siman 12

- **séif 1, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים עַל כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד בִּפְנֵי עַצְמוֹ » → upon each other severed thread of the ד׳ long threads, upon each one by itself
- **séif 1, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם לֹא נִפְסְקוּ אֶלָּא ז׳ חוּטִין … כָּשֵׁר » → And even if only ז׳ threads were severed, the severed thread of the ד׳ long threads must have ג׳ פְּעָמִים כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה upon one [single] thread; if it has this measure — kosher.
- **séif 1, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « בְּכָל חוּטִים הַפְּסוּקִים מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים … מִכָּל חוּטִין הַפְּסוּקִים מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים » → even if there remains in all the severed threads of the ד׳ long threads only enough to make, from all the severed threads of the ד׳ long threads, a single עֲנִיבָה of all together
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים עַל כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד בִּפְנֵי עַצְמוֹ » → sur chaque autre fil coupé des ד׳ fils longs, sur chacun séparément
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם לֹא נִפְסְקוּ אֶלָּא ז׳ חוּטִין — צָרִיךְ לִהְיוֹת בַּחוּט הַנִּפְסָק מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים ג׳ פְּעָמִים כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה עַל חוּט [אֶחָד], וְאִם יֵשׁ בּוֹ כְּשִׁעוּר הַזֶּה — כָּשֵׁר » → Et même si seulement ז׳ fils se sont coupés, il faut que le fil coupé des ד׳ fils longs ait ג׳ פְּעָמִים כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה sur un [seul] fil ; s'il a cette mesure — casher. (la phrase entière n'avait aucune traduction ; il fallait la traduire pour rendre les mots rétablis)
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « בְּכָל חוּטִים הַפְּסוּקִים מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים … מִכָּל חוּטִין הַפְּסוּקִים מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים » → même s'il ne reste, dans tous les fils coupés des ד׳ fils longs, que de quoi faire, de tous les fils coupés des ד׳ fils longs, une seule עֲנִיבָה de tous ensemble
- **séif 1, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « עַל כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד בִּפְנֵי עַצְמוֹ ; phrase des ז׳ חוּטִין ; מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים (×3) » → על כל אחד ואחד משאר החוטין הפסוקין בפני עצמו (… בחוט הנפסק מד׳ הארוכים) … ואפילו לא נפסקו אלא ז׳ חוטין — צריך שיהיה בחוט הנפסק מד׳ הארוכים ג׳ פְּעָמִים כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה על חוט אחד, ואם יש בו שיעור זה — כָּשֵׁר. וְיֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים שאפילו לא נשתייר בכל החוטין הפסוקים מד׳ הארוכים אלא כדי לעשות מכל החוטין הפסוקים מד׳ הארוכים עֲנִיבָה אַחַת מִכֻּלָּם יַחַד
- **séif 2, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 2 entier (mêmes passages que FR) » → … at the time of attaching the tsitsit to the garment … because there remains of each severed thread its second head, which has even more than כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה. Moreover, even if all eight threads were severed — four of them entirely, while the other four, on the second side of the knot, keep only כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — kosher: since there remains of each of the ד׳ long threads כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, nothing more is needed. When does this apply? When all the severed threads were on one side of the knot. But if they were on both sides — even if only two of the eight threads were severed, one on this side and one on that side, and no כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remained in them — or even if … one did not take care, when making the tsitsit, that the ד׳ רָאשִׁים always lie together on one side of the knot … therefore the tsitsit are pasoul, for one of the ד׳ threads that must be in the tsitsit מִן הַתּוֹרָה is missing. And if neither of the two severed threads has כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה by itself, but joining the two remnants would make כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — even so one should invalidate: although כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remains of the severed long thread, nevertheless, since neither of the heads of this severed thread that emerge beyond the knot has כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה by itself, they are as if they did not exist at all … it is thus as if nothing at all remained of the long thread.
- **séif 2, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 2 entier (corps de la traduction réécrit pour rendre tout le texte rétabli : בַּבֶּגֶד, הֲרֵי, la phrase וְלֹא עוֹד…, בַּמֶּה דְּבָרִים אֲמוּרִים…, les deux cas de pesoul détaillés, la fin אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁמִּחוּט הָאָרֹךְ… כְּאִלּוּ לֹא נִשְׁתַּיֵּר כְּלוּם) » → … à la pose du tsitsit sur le vêtement … car il reste de chaque fil coupé sa seconde tête, qui a même plus que כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה. Bien plus : même si les huit fils se sont coupés, quatre d'entre eux entièrement, et que les quatre autres, du second côté du nœud, ne gardent que כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — casher : puisqu'il reste de chacun des ד׳ fils longs כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, il n'en faut pas davantage. À quoi cela s'applique-t-il ? Quand tous les fils coupés étaient d'un même côté du nœud. Mais s'ils étaient des deux côtés — même si deux fils seulement des huit se sont coupés, un de ce côté-ci et un de ce côté-là, sans qu'il y reste כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה —, ou même si les deux fils coupés étaient d'un même côté du nœud mais qu'on n'a pas pris soin, en faisant le tsitsit, que les ד׳ רָאשִׁים soient toujours placés ensemble d'un même côté du nœud, il y a lieu de craindre … c'est pourquoi le tsitsit est pasoul, car il manque un des ד׳ fils qui doivent être dans le tsitsit מִן הַתּוֹרָה. Et si chacun des deux fils coupés, pris seul, n'a pas כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, mais qu'en joignant les deux restes on aurait כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — même ainsi il faut déclarer pasoul : bien qu'il reste du fil long coupé כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, néanmoins, puisque aucune des têtes de ce fil coupé qui sortent hors du nœud n'a, seule, כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, elles sont comme si elles n'existaient pas du tout, car tout ce qui est moindre que כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה est comme s'il n'était pas ; c'est donc comme s'il ne restait rien du fil long.
- **séif 2, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 2 entier (mêmes passages que FR) » → … וְדִקְדֵּק בעת הטלת הציצית בבגד … שהרי נשתייר מכל חוט וחוט שנפסק הרֹאשׁ השני, שיש בו אף יותר מכדי עניבה. ולא עוד, אלא אף אם נפסקו כל הח׳ חוטין — ד׳ מהם לגמרי, וד׳ האחרים שבצד השני של הקשר לא נשתייר בהם אלא כדי עניבה — כָּשֵׁר: כיון שנשתייר מכל חוט מד׳ הארוכים כדי עניבה, אין צריך יותר. במה דברים אמורים? כשהיו כל הפסוקין מצד אחד של הקשר. אבל אם היו מִב׳ הצדדים — אף לא נפסקו אלא ב׳ חוטין מהשמונה, אחד מצד זה ואחד מצד זה … ולכן הציצית פסולים … ואם אין בכל אחד מב׳ הנפסקים לבדו כדי עניבה, אף שבצירוף ב׳ השיורים יש כדי עניבה — אף על פי כן יש לפסול: אף שמן החוט הארוך שנפסק נשתייר כדי עניבה, מכל מקום כיון שבכל אחד מראשי חוט זה היוצאים חוץ לקשר אין כדי עניבה לבדו, הרי הם כאילו לא היו כלל … ונמצא כאילו לא נשתייר כלום מן החוט הארוך.
- **séif 3, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 3 entier (mêmes passages que FR) » → … nevertheless, for this completion, severed threads keeping כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה suffice, since at the time of attaching the tsitsit to the garment all the threads were whole; but the ב׳ חוּטִין that are עִקַּר הַמִּצְוָה, which had to be present even in the time when there was תכלת, these threads must always remain whole; and then one validates for the other threads, if they were severed and כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remained in each of the two severed long threads. Therefore, if three heads of the eight heads were severed … for there is cause to fear that no two of the severed heads come from one long thread, but each one by itself comes from a long thread … But if only two heads were severed, if enough remained in one of them to make a small עֲנִיבָה — even if one cannot insert other threads into the עֲנִיבָה, but only that very thread with which the עֲנִיבָה is made — kosher … even if the two heads were severed entirely and כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remained in neither of them — kosher, because these two heads certainly come from two long threads; thus more than כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remained in each of the two long threads, namely the whole second head.
- **séif 3, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 3 entier (corps réécrit : מִכָּל מָקוֹם ; כֵּיוָן דְּבִשְׁעַת הֲטָלַת צִיצִית לַבֶּגֶד הָיוּ כָּל הַחוּטִין שְׁלֵמִים ; וְאָז מַכְשִׁירִים בִּשְׁאָר הַחוּטִין… ; la crainte des ג׳ רָאשִׁים en entier ; אֲפִלּוּ אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לְהַכְנִיס תּוֹךְ הָעֲנִיבָה חוּטִין אֲחֵרִים… ; les deux dernières branches en entier) » → … néanmoins, pour ce complément, il suffit de fils coupés gardant כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, puisqu'au moment de la pose du tsitsit sur le vêtement tous les fils étaient entiers ; mais les ב׳ חוּטִין qui sont עִקַּר הַמִּצְוָה, et qui devaient être là même au temps où il y avait le תכלת, ces fils-là doivent toujours rester entiers ; et alors on rend casher pour les autres fils, s'ils se sont coupés et qu'il reste כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה dans chacun des deux fils longs coupés. Par conséquent, si trois têtes des huit têtes se sont coupées … car il y a lieu de craindre que deux des têtes coupées ne sortent pas d'un même fil long, mais que chacune sorte, à elle seule, d'un fil long ; et alors il ne reste pas deux fils longs entiers. Mais si deux têtes seulement se sont coupées, s'il reste dans l'une d'elles de quoi faire une petite עֲנִיבָה — même si l'on ne peut faire entrer dans la עֲנִיבָה d'autres fils, mais seulement ce fil-là avec lequel on fait la עֲנִיבָה — casher … Mais s'il ne reste dans aucune des deux têtes coupées de quoi faire une petite עֲנִיבָה : si ces deux têtes … l'une de ce côté-ci et l'autre de ce côté-là, ou même si toutes deux sortent du même côté mais qu'on n'a pas pris soin … — pasoul … il n'y a ici que ג׳ fils longs, et il n'y a pas de tsitsit à moins de ד׳ fils, comme on l'a expliqué au siman יא. Mais si ces deux têtes sortent d'un même côté du nœud et qu'en faisant le tsitsit on a pris soin de marquer les ד׳ רָאשִׁים … même si les deux têtes se sont coupées entièrement sans qu'il reste dans aucune כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — casher, car ces deux têtes sortent certainement de deux fils longs ; il reste donc dans chacun des deux fils longs plus que כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, à savoir la seconde tête, entière.
- **séif 3, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « séif 3 entier (mêmes passages que FR) » → … מכל מקום להשלמה זו דַּי בחוטין פסוקים שיש בהם כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, שבשעת הטלת הציצית לבגד היו כל החוטין שלמים; אבל ב׳ חוּטִין שהם עִקַּר הַמִּצְוָה, שהיו צריכים להיות אף בזמן שהיה התכלת — צריכין לעולם בִּשְׁלֵמוּת, ואז מכשירים בשאר החוטין אם נפסקו ונשתייר כדי עניבה בכל אחד מב׳ הארוכים הנפסקים. … שיש לחוש שמא אין ב׳ מהראשים שנפסקו יוצאים מחוט ארוך אחד, אלא כל אחד יוצא מחוט ארוך אחר … אף שאינו יכול להכניס בתוכה חוטין אחרים אלא אותו החוט לבדו — כָּשֵׁר … אבל אם שניהם מצד אחד של הקשר, ודקדק … אף נפסקו ב׳ הראשים לגמרי ולא נשתייר באף אחד מהם כדי עניבה — כָּשֵׁר, שבוודאי יוצאים מב׳ חוטין ארוכים, ונשתייר בכל אחד מהם יותר מכדי עניבה, דהיינו הראש השני השלם.
- **séif 6, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « mêmes passages que FR séif 6 » → even if severed where they are coiled in the גָּדִיל, and only כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remains in them from the point of severance to where they emerge from the corner (כָּנָף), i.e. the start of the גְּדִיל — kosher ; … only כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה remains of them ; (e.g. if 3 חֻלְיוֹת of the גְּדִיל remain, undo 2 חֻלְיוֹת of it and leave one חוּלְיָא, so that there be one third גָּדִיל, two thirds עָנָף) ; (the first view of this séif) ; but if it is impossible otherwise (e.g. where …) ; yet one does not recite the ברכה over these tsitsit
- **séif 6, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « בָּהֶם מִמָּקוֹם שֶׁנִּפְסְקוּ עַד מָקוֹם שֶׁיּוֹצְאִים מִן הַכָּנָף דְּהַיְנוּ הַתְחָלַת הַגְּדִיל ; מֵהֶם ; מִן הַגְּדִיל … מִמֶּנּוּ ב׳ חֻלְיוֹת … חוּלְיָא … כְּדֵי ; שֶׁבְּסָעִיף זֶה ; בְּעִנְיָן אַחֵר ; עַל צִיצִית אֵלּוּ » → que même s'ils se sont coupés là où ils sont enroulés dans le גָּדִיל, et qu'il ne leur reste, du point de coupure jusqu'à l'endroit où ils sortent du coin (כָּנָף), c'est-à-dire le début du גְּדִיל, que כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — casher ; … et qu'il n'en reste que … ; (ex. s'il reste du גְּדִיל 3 חֻלְיוֹת, on en défait 2 חֻלְיוֹת et on laisse une חוּלְיָא, afin qu'il y ait un tiers גָּדִיל, deux tiers עָנָף) ; (le premier avis de ce séif) ; mais si c'est impossible autrement (par exemple là où …) ; toutefois on ne bénit pas sur ces tsitsit
- **séif 6, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « mêmes passages que FR séif 6 » → ולא נשתייר בהם ממקום הפסיקה עד מקום שיוצאים מן הכנף, דהיינו תחילת הגָּדִיל, אלא כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — כָּשֵׁר ; (כגון אם נשתיירו מן הגדיל ג׳ חֻלְיוֹת — יתיר מהן ב׳ חוליות ויניח חוליא אחת, כדי שיהיה שליש גדיל ושני שלישי ענף) ; (כסברא הראשונה שבסעיף זה) ; ובאי אפשר בענין אחר (כגון במקום שאין ציצית מצויים) ; לֹא יְבָרֵךְ על ציצית אלו
- **séif 7, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « mêmes passages que FR séif 7 » → or only in part, the rest of it still remaining twisted ; we regard that thread (or its part undone from its twisting) as if that thread (or that part) were entirely severed ; of each one of the ד׳ long threads
- **séif 7, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « מִקְצָת הַחוּט וּמִקְצָתוֹ עוֹמֵד עֲדַיִן בִּשְׁזִירָתוֹ ; מִשְּׁזִירָתוֹ … אוֹתוֹ הַחוּט אוֹ מִקְצָתוֹ ; מִכָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד מִד׳ חוּטִין הָאֲרֻכִּים » → ou en partie seulement, l'autre partie restant encore dans sa torsion ; on considère ce fil (ou sa partie défaite de sa torsion) comme si ce fil (ou cette partie) était coupé entièrement ; de chacun des ד׳ fils longs
- **séif 7, HE** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « mêmes passages que FR séif 7 » → בין מקצתו בלבד ומקצתו עומד עדיין בשזירתו ; רואים אותו החוט או מקצתו שנתפרק משזירתו כְּאִלּוּ נִפְסָק לְגַמְרֵי ; מכל אחד ואחד מד׳ חוטין הָאֲרֻכִּים

### Siman 13

- **séif 1, EN** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici (traduite de l'hébreu)) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ נִפְסְלוּ כָּל הַד׳ צִיצִית … וְלָכֵן אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁמְּחֻבָּרִים לַטַּלִּית — אֵינָן בְּטֵלִים … שֶׁהֲרֵי אֵין לַטַּלִּית צֹרֶךְ בָּהֶם כְּלָל » → And even if all four tsitsit have become pasoul through the cutting of threads, with no כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה left in them, the threads that remained whole and uncut are still important in his eyes, for he intends to add other threads to them; therefore, &lt;strong&gt;though attached to the talit, they are not בָּטֵל&lt;/strong&gt; to it and are like a burden, since, being now pasoul, they are neither נוֹי nor תשמיש of the talit, &lt;strong&gt;for the talit has no need of them at all&lt;/strong&gt;.
- **séif 1, EN** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici) — « אֶלָּא הוּא צָרִיךְ לַעֲשׂוֹת צִיצִית אֲחֵרִים תַּחְתֵּיהֶם » → so that all is pasoul, with no remedy other than making other tsitsit in their place
- **séif 1, EN** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici) — « וּלְפִיכָךְ מֻתָּר » → therefore one may go out with it
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (la phrase « ואפילו נפסלו… », présente dans le sa-he mais réduite dans l'ancienne traduction à « ils ne sont ni נוי ni תשמיש du talit », a dû être rendue en entier pour y placer les deux passages rétablis)) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ נִפְסְלוּ כָּל הַד׳ צִיצִית … וְדַעְתּוֹ לְהוֹסִיף עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד חוּטִין אֲחֵרִים, וְלָכֵן אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁמְּחֻבָּרִים לַטַּלִּית — אֵינָן בְּטֵלִים לְגַבֵּי הַטַּלִּית וַהֲרֵי הֵם כְּמַשְׁאוֹי, כֵּיוָן שֶׁעַכְשָׁו הֵם פְּסוּלִים אֵינָן לֹא מִנּוֹי הַטַּלִּית וְלֹא מִתַּשְׁמִישֵׁי הַטַּלִּית, שֶׁהֲרֵי אֵין לַטַּלִּית צֹרֶךְ בָּהֶם כְּלָל » → Et même si les quatre tsitsit sont devenus pesoulim par la coupure des fils, sans qu'il y reste כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה, les fils restés entiers, non coupés, sont encore importants à ses yeux, car il compte leur ajouter d'autres fils ; c'est pourquoi, &lt;strong&gt;bien qu'attachés au talit, ils ne sont pas בָּטֵל&lt;/strong&gt; à son égard et sont comme un fardeau, puisque, étant maintenant pesoulim, ils ne sont ni נוֹי ni תשמיש du talit, &lt;strong&gt;car le talit n'en a aucun besoin&lt;/strong&gt;.
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « אֶלָּא הוּא צָרִיךְ לַעֲשׂוֹת צִיצִית אֲחֵרִים תַּחְתֵּיהֶם » → de sorte que tout est pasoul et sans réparation, sinon de faire d'autres tsitsit à leur place
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu) — « וּלְפִיכָךְ מֻתָּר » → c'est pourquoi il est permis
- **séif 1, HE** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici, et resserrée sur les mots de la source) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ נִפְסְלוּ כָּל הַד׳ צִיצִית … וְלָכֵן אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁמְּחֻבָּרִים לַטַּלִּית — אֵינָן בְּטֵלִים … שֶׁהֲרֵי אֵין לַטַּלִּית צֹרֶךְ בָּהֶם כְּלָל » → ואפילו נפסלו כל הד׳ ציצית בפסיקת החוטין שלא נשתייר בהם כְּדֵי עֲנִיבָה — החוטין שנשתיירו שלמים עדיין חשובים בעיניו ודעתו להוסיף עליהם חוטין אחרים, ולכן &lt;strong&gt;אף שמחוברים לטלית אינן בטלים&lt;/strong&gt; והרי הם כמשאוי, שעכשיו פסולים הם ואינם לא נוֹי ולא תשמיש הטלית, &lt;strong&gt;שהרי אין לטלית צורך בהם כלל&lt;/strong&gt;.
- **séif 1, HE** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici) — « אֶלָּא הוּא צָרִיךְ לַעֲשׂוֹת צִיצִית אֲחֵרִים תַּחְתֵּיהֶם » → שכולם פסולים ואין להם תקנה אלא לעשות ציצית אחרים תחתיהם
- **séif 1, HE** (portée depuis la phrase FR ajoutée ici) — « וּלְפִיכָךְ מֻתָּר » → ולפיכך מותר לצאת

### Siman 276

- **séif 7, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 7), au registre de la page EN) — « וְאַף אִם סָפֵק לְאֵיזֶה מֵהֶם מַדְלִיק — הוֹלְכִים לְהַחֲמִיר בְּסָפֵק זֶה. וְכָל זֶה כְּשֶׁמַּדְלִיק סְתָם … וְכֵן אִם יֵשׁ הוֹכָחָה שֶׁמַּדְלִיק בִּשְׁבִיל נָכְרִי אַחֵר. אֲבָל אִם יֵשׁ הוֹכָחָה שֶׁמַּדְלִיק לְצֹרֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל וּלְצֹרֶךְ נָכְרִי, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהָרֹב נָכְרִים — אָסוּר » → And even if it is in doubt for which of them he kindles — we go to be stringent in this doubt.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And all this is when he kindles without specification, for ordinarily his intention is for the diners; but if there is evidence that he kindles for himself — for example, we see that he uses its light (immediately) — even though the majority are Yisraelim, it is permitted to use its light. And likewise if there is evidence that he kindles for another akum.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;But if there is evidence that he kindles for the need of a Yisrael and for the need of an akum, even though the majority are akumim — it is forbidden:
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 8), ajustee sur l'hebreu : « שֶׁרַבּוּ הָאוֹמְרִים » rendu « many are those who say » (la FR dit « la majorité dit »)) — « וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם, אִם נֵר אֶחָד דָּלוּק עֲדַיִן … (וְאִם הִדְלִיק מֵעַצְמוֹ — מֻתָּר לֵהָנוֹת מִמֶּנּוּ …) אֲבָל כָּל אָדָם צָרִיךְ לְהַחֲמִיר לְעַצְמוֹ … לְפִי שֶׁרַבּוּ הָאוֹמְרִים … וְדִבְרֵיהֶם עִקָּר … שֶׁמּוּטָב שֶׁיִּהְיוּ שׁוֹגְגִים וְאַל יִהְיוּ מְזִידִים » → Nevertheless, if one ner is still burning and they can eat by it — it is forbidden to tell an akum to kindle another, for there is not so much of a mitzvah here to override the shvus of amirah le-akum on its account.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;(And if he kindled of his own accord — it is permitted to benefit from it even after the first has gone out, since oneg Shabbos depends on this, as will be explained.)&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;But every person must be stringent with himself not to tell an akum to kindle a ner even though he sits in the dark and cannot eat the Shabbos meal, because many are those who say that they did not permit a shvus in the case of a mitzvah except a shvus de-shvus, as will be explained in siman 307 — and their words are the main view. Therefore one should not be lenient in this at all, even for a great need; but one should not protest against those who are lenient, for it is better that they be unintentional transgressors than intentional ones:
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 9) ; « בֵּין אֲצִילֵי יָדָיו » rendu « between his elbows » (FR : « entre les jointures de ses bras »)) — « כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן רע״ט, וְכָל מֻקְצֶה מֻתָּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ בְּטִלְטוּל מִן הַצַּד … שֶׁלֹּא אָסְרוּ לַעֲשׂוֹת עַל יְדֵי נָכְרִי בְּשַׁבָּת אֶלָּא דְּבָרִים שֶׁאֵין שׁוּם הֶתֵּר לְיִשְׂרָאֵל לַעֲשׂוֹת בְּעַצְמוֹ. וְאֵין לְהוֹרוֹת הֶתֵּר זֶה אֶלָּא לִבְנֵי תוֹרָה … וְיָקֵלּוּ יוֹתֵר » → , as will be explained in siman 279; and any &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; may be moved by a &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; (indirect moving), as will be explained in siman 311. And since the Yisrael himself could have brought the ner here permissibly by a &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; — for example with the backs of his hands, or between his elbows, and the like — when the akum brings it in a forbidden way, by a complete moving, there is nothing to it, for they forbade having an akum do on Shabbos only things that the Yisrael has no permitted way at all to do himself.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And one should not teach this leniency except to &lt;em&gt;bnei Torah&lt;/em&gt;; but for &lt;em&gt;amei ha-aretz&lt;/em&gt; one should not permit it, lest they become accustomed to amirah le-akum, and compare one thing to another, and be more lenient:
- **séif 10, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 10), recalee sur l'hebreu la ou la FR resume (« וְאִם כֵּן זֶה הַנֵּר אִם יְטַלְטְלֶנּוּ עַל יְדֵי דָּבָר אַחֵר … » rendu litteralement)) — « לְפִי שֶׁטִּלְטוּל זֶה הוּא לְצֹרֵךְ הַנֵּר שֶׁהוּא מֻקְצֶה … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן שי״א. (וְאַף שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר שָׁם שֶׁכָּל טִלְטוּל מִן הַצַּד שֶׁהוּא עַל יְדֵי גוּפוֹ … וְאֵין לְהָקֵל יוֹתֵר מִמַּה שֶּׁהֵם עַצְמָם מְקִלִּים) » → , for this moving is for the need of the ner, which is &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt;, and a Yisrael has no permission to move &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; even by a &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; when his moving is for the sake of the &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; itself, as will be explained in siman 311.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;(And although it will be explained there that any &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; done with his body, not with his hands, he may move it even for the need of the &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt;, while any other &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt;, done by means of another object in his hands, is permitted only when he moves it for the need of its place, but not when he needs to use the &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; itself — and if so, this ner, were he to move it by means of another object in his hands, he could not move it in order to benefit from its light here, in the place to which he brings it, and it is also altogether impossible to bring it by a &lt;em&gt;tiltul min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; with another object in his hands; and if he moves it with his body, for example with the backs of his hands or between his elbows and the like, he may move it even so that it not be stolen — nevertheless one should not permit telling an akum to put it away, because those very authorities who permit moving in the normal way through an akum when the Yisrael has a permission of &lt;em&gt;min ha-tzad&lt;/em&gt; hold that even with his body there is no permission to move for the need of the &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; itself, but only in order to use the &lt;em&gt;muktzeh&lt;/em&gt; itself, or because he needs its place; and one should not be more lenient than they themselves are lenient):
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 11)) — « וּלְפִיכָךְ (צָרִיךְ לִמְחוֹת בָּם שֶׁלֹּא יַדְלִיקוּ אִם הַנֵּר שֶׁלּוֹ, וְאִם לֹא מִחָה בָּהֶם וְהִדְלִיקוּהוּ) — אָסוּר לֵילֵךְ עִמָּהֶם לַחוּץ … אֲפִלּוּ הַנֵּר שֶׁל נָכְרִי. (וְאִם מִחָה בָּם וְלֹא הִשְׁגִּיחוּ בּוֹ וְהִדְלִיקוּ — מֻתָּר לְהִשְׁתַּמֵּשׁ לְאוֹרוֹ אִם אֵינוֹ מַעֲרִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר לְמַעְלָה) » → ; and therefore (he must protest against them that they not kindle, if the ner is his; and if he did not protest against them and they kindled it) — it is forbidden to go outside with them until they put the ner down in the house, so that he not benefit from the light of the ner that was kindled for the sake of his going, even if the ner belongs to the akum.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;(And if he protested against them and they did not heed him and kindled — it is permitted to use its light if he is not acting with &lt;em&gt;ha'aramah&lt;/em&gt;, as was explained above):
- **séif 13, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 13) ; « מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם » rendu « from while it was still day » (registre EN de la page) et non « depuis la veille » ; « [Yisrael] » ajoute entre crochets pour « זֶה ») — « אִם יִהְיֶה מֻתָּר לוֹ לְהִתְחַמֵּם בָּהּ. וְאֵינָהּ דּוֹמָה לְנֵר … שֶׁחִמּוּם לְאֶחָד חִמּוּם לְמֵאָה. וְיֵשׁ מַתִּירִין אֲפִלּוּ בִּמְדוּרָה … (רַק שֶׁלְּכַתְּחִלָּה צָרִיךְ לִזָּהֵר …) וּלְעֵת הַצֹּרֶךְ יֵשׁ לִסְמוֹךְ עַל דִּבְרֵיהֶם לְהָקֵל בְּדִבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים » →  if it were permitted for him to warm himself by it. And it is not comparable to a ner, where there is no concern that he will add for his sake, for a ner for one is a ner for a hundred — which is not so with a medurah, for the more people warm themselves by it, the more wood he must add to it to enlarge it. But if he heated the winter-house for his own need or for the need of a sick person — it is permitted for a healthy person to go from the summer-house to the winter-house to warm himself in it, for there is no concern that he will heat it more for his sake, for heating for one is heating for a hundred.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And some permit even with a medurah, and do not decree lest he add for his sake, because even if he did not add for his sake he would benefit somewhat from the medurah. And although through the additional wood he benefits from it amply — this additional benefit does not warrant a decree on its account lest he tell him to add wood for his sake.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And even if the akum made a small medurah for his own need only, or for the need of a sick person, and afterwards this [Yisrael] came to warm himself by it, and the akum added wood for his sake of his own accord — this addition does not forbid him to warm himself there, since he could have warmed himself there somewhat even without this addition.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And likewise, if the medurah was burning from while it was still day, and on Shabbos the akum added wood to it for the sake of a Yisrael — it is permitted to warm oneself by it.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;(Only that a priori one must take care in all this to protest him if the wood belongs to the Yisrael, as has been explained.)&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And at a time of need one may rely on their words to be lenient in &lt;em&gt;divrei soferim&lt;/em&gt;:
- **séif 14, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 14)) — « וְכֵן אִם הָיָה נֵר דּוֹלֵק מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם … וְאַחַר כָּךְ אָסוּר. וְיֵשׁ חוֹלְקִין עַל זֶה וּמַתִּירִין … לֹא יָצָא מִדֵּי הֶתֵּרוֹ לְעוֹלָם … וּבִמְקוֹם צֹרֶךְ גָּדוֹל, דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁעֹנֶג שַׁבָּת תָּלוּי בְּזֶה — יֵשׁ לִסְמוֹךְ עַל דִּבְרֵיהֶם לְהָקֵל » →  And likewise, if a ner was burning from while it was still day, or for the need of an akum or a sick person, and an akum came and added another ner for the sake of a Yisrael — there is permission to use its light only while the first ner is burning; but after the first has gone out, it is forbidden to use the light of the second. And likewise, if he added oil to the burning ner — there is permission to use its light only until the oil that was in the ner at first would have been consumed, and after that it is forbidden.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And some disagree with this and permit — whether with a medurah, with a ner, or with the addition of oil — even though they belong to a Yisrael: since at the outset, when the akum did the forbidden act for the sake of the Yisrael, it was permitted to benefit from it, it never leaves its permitted status, even after the first wood, or the first ner, or the first oil has been consumed.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And in a case of great need, namely that oneg Shabbos depends on it — one may rely on their words to be lenient:
- **séif 15, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 15)) — « וְכֵן לוֹמַר לוֹ לְחַמֵּם אֶת בֵּית הַחֹרֶף … אָסוּר לוֹמַר לְנָכְרִי לַעֲשׂוֹת מְדוּרָה אוֹ לְחַמֵּם בֵּית הַחֹרֶף. וְאִם עוֹשֶׂה מְדוּרָה מֵעַצְמוֹ מֵעֲצֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל … אֲבָל אִם הוּא דָּר בּוֹ — אֵין צָרִיךְ לָצֵאת מִמֶּנּוּ » → , and likewise to tell him to heat the winter-house, for all are like sick people in the face of great cold. But on a day when the cold is not so great, if he has no small children who suffer greatly — it is forbidden to tell an akum to make a medurah or to heat the winter-house.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And if he makes a medurah of his own accord from the Yisrael's wood — he must protest him. And if he did not protest — it is forbidden to warm oneself by it.&lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And if he heated the winter-house of his own accord for the sake of a Yisrael with the Yisrael's wood — it is forbidden to enter it in order to warm oneself. But if he lives in it — he need not leave it:
- **séif 17, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (sa-fr du seif 17), inseree a sa place entre « intentional ones. » et « And in any event, ») — « וְאַף בְּיוֹם שֶׁהַקֹּר גָּדוֹל אֵין לְחַמֵּם בֵּית הַחֹרֶף שְׁנֵי פְעָמִים בְּיוֹם הַשַּׁבָּת, כִּי דַּי בַּמֶּה שֶׁהִסִּיק הַתַּנּוּר פַּעַם רִאשׁוֹן בַּבֹּקֶר » → &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;And even on a day of great cold one should not heat the winter-house twice on the Shabbos day, for what he heated the oven the first time in the morning suffices.

### Siman 309

- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְאֵינוֹ דוֹמֶה לִשְׁאָר טִלְטוּל דָּבָר הָאָסוּר שֶׁמִּטַּלְטֵל מֵאֵלָיו … אֲבָל כַּאן אֵין זֶה צֹרֶךְ כָּל כָּךְ לִטּוֹל אֶת הַתִּינוֹק כְּשֶׁאֵין לוֹ גַּעֲגוּעִין עָלָיו » → And this is not like other tiltul of a forbidden object that moves by itself through one's moving with his hand the permitted object that is with it, which the Sages permitted when it is impossible to shake (lena'er) the forbidden off the permitted, as will be explained — for they permitted this only when one needs the permitted object; but here there is no such need to take the child when he has no longing for him:
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (les deux autres omissions du séif 3, « כשהוא מהדק בה » et « שאינו נופל ממנה כשממלאים בה », étaient déjà rendues dans l'anglais existant)) — « וְאִם לָאו — אָסוּר לְמַלֹּאת בָּהּ, אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן יִחֵד אֶבֶן זֶה לְכָךְ לְעוֹלָם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר לְעֵיל בְּסִמָּן ש״ח » → Otherwise — it is forbidden to draw with it, unless one has designated this stone for this purpose permanently, as was explained above in siman 308:
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְכֵן אִם נַעֲשָׂה בָּסִיס לְאִסּוּר וּלְהֶתֵּר … מֻתָּר לְטַלְטֵל הַהֶתֵּר עִם הָאִסּוּר שֶׁעָלָיו » → And likewise, if it has become a basis for both issur and heter — for example, if while it was still day he also placed upon it an object permitted in tiltul that is more important than the forbidden object, as will be explained in siman 310 — since it has not become a basis to issur alone, it still remains in its permitted status, and it is permitted to move it, even though the issur upon it also moves along with it by itself. / However, if it is possible to shake the issur off it immediately — he must shake it off first, and afterward move the heter alone to the place where he needs it. / And if it is not possible to shake it off, or if there would be some loss to the permitted object were he to shake off the issur, or if the shaking would not suffice for him to accomplish the need for which he requires this tiltul — for example, he moves the heter with the issur upon it to another place because he needs to clear the spot on which they now are, in order to use it for some use or other need, such that if he shook off the issur here he could not accomplish his need in this place — it is permitted to move the heter with the issur upon it:
- **séif 5, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « שֶׁאִם יְנַעֲרֵן יִתְטַנְּפוּ בַּקַּרְקַע וְיִפָּסְדוּ … וּלְהוֹלִיכָהּ לְמָקוֹם שֶׁצְּרִיכִים לוֹ הַפֵּרוֹת לְאָכְלָם שָׁם … מֻתָּר לוֹ לִטְּלָהּ מִכַּאן כְּמוֹת שֶׁהִיא בְּכָל עִנְיָן » → which, if shaken out, would get soiled on the ground and be ruined [remplace « that would be ruined if shaken out »] … and to carry it to the place where he needs the fruits, to eat them there. And he is not obliged to take the fruits in his hands and carry them, lest they fall from his hands to the ground and get soiled. / And if they are placed in small baskets inside the basket — he should take the small baskets by hand out of the basket and carry them one by one, in such a way that there is no concern that the fruits fall to the ground. / And if he needs to take the basket itself to another place in order to use it there for some use — he should take the small baskets out of it by hand and put them on the ground, and afterward shake the stone out of it and take it. / And if they are dry fruits, which are not ruined when they get soiled on the ground, even though they are not in small baskets — he should not take the basket as it is with the fruits and the stone, whether he needs the fruits or needs the basket; rather, he should shake them out of it together with the stone, and afterward gather them by hand and put them back into the basket if he wishes. / But if he needs the place of the basket, such that the shaking would not suffice for him — he is permitted to take it from here as it is, in any manner:
- **séif 6, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, sauf « to tilt it there » (לְהַטּוֹתָהּ שָׁם), que le français condense en « pour y faire tomber la pierre » : traduit de l'hebreu) — « וּמְנַעֵר אֶת הַכַּר וְהַמָּעוֹת נוֹפְלִים … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן שי״א » → and he shakes the pillow and the coins fall off. / And if the barrel was among the barrels, such that he cannot tilt it in its place — he may lift it as it is, with the stone on it, and carry it to another place to tilt it there in order to make the stone fall off it. / And if he needs the place of the barrel and the pillow, such that the shaking does not suffice for him — he may move them as they are, with the stone and the coins on them, in order to clear their place. / And if he needs neither the pillow itself nor its place, but fears that the coins on it will be stolen — it is forbidden even to shake it so that the coins fall beneath it, since [the Sages] permitted tiltul via another object only when one moves it for the sake of a permitted object, and not for the sake of a forbidden object, as will be explained in siman 311:
- **séif 7, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « בֵּין שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לִמְקוֹם הֶחָבִית וְהַכַּר … וְאָסוּר לְטַלְטְלָן כְּלָל … וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם הָיָה בְּדַעְתּוֹ … וְיֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים … וְהָעִקָּר כִּסְבָרָא הָרִאשׁוֹנָה … כְּגוֹן אִם נָפַל נֵר עַל הַשֻּׁלְחָן, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן רע״ז » → — whether he needs the place of the barrel and the pillow or needs them themselves, to take the wine from the barrel and to lie on the pillow — … and it is forbidden to move them at all, even by mere tilting and shaking, like the forbidden object itself. / And even if he intended that they not remain there all of Shabbos but only at the entry of Shabbos — that is, he intended that they remain there only until after bein hashemashos, when he would remove them from there by shaking — this is of no avail at all to permit him even the shaking he had in mind, since, once the barrel and the pillow became a basis le-issur during bein hashemashos, which is the beginning of the entry of Shabbos, they were set aside for all of Shabbos, and his intent does not avail to permit them. / And some say (yesh omrim) that they become a basis le-issur only if he intended that the stone and the coins remain there all of Shabbos; but if he intended to take them from there in the middle of Shabbos, whether through a non-Jew or by shaking — the barrel and the pillow did not become a basis for them at all, even at the beginning of the entry of Shabbos, and they were not forbidden in tiltul at all, nor set aside for all of Shabbos. Therefore, if he changed his mind, even at the beginning of the entry of Shabbos, to tilt the barrel and shake the pillow, if he needed them themselves, or to move them as they are with the stone and the coins on them, if he needed their place — this is permitted, as in the case of forgetfulness. / But if he did not intend to take the stone and the coins off the barrel and the pillow by shaking or through a non-Jew, but rather intended to move, in the middle of Shabbos, the barrel and the pillow with the stone and the coins on them to another place, through a non-Jew or through a Jew, when he would need their place — this is of no avail at all, since even in that place the stone and the coins will be resting on them: they are a basis for them. / And the main ruling (ikar) follows the first opinion. Nevertheless, in a case of loss (hefsed), one may rely on the latter opinion — for example, if a lamp fell on the table, as was explained in siman 277:
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « כְּמוֹ כְּשֶׁהָיָה הָאִסּוּר עֲלֵיהֶן … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן ש״י » → as when the issur was on them … as will be explained in siman 310:
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « בֵּין לְצֹרֶךְ הָאִסּוּר עַצְמוֹ … וְדִינוֹ כְּשׁוֹכֵחַ … לְפִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ צָרִיךְ לוֹ שֶׁתְּהֵא הַמְּנוֹרָה עַל הַמַּפָּה … וְלֹא נֶאֶסְרָה בְּטִלְטוּל » → — whether for the need of the issur itself, so that it rest upon it securely, such as coins on the pillow, or for the need of another, permitted object, such as a stone on the mouth of the barrel. But if he placed it upon it casually, not intentionally for some need that requires it to rest upon it, but as people commonly put objects one upon another in a chest because one has no room to clear a place for each object at the bottom of the chest — this has not become a basis for that, and its law is as in the case of forgetfulness. … since he does not need the menorah to be on the cloth but on the table, and he stands it on the cloth only because he cannot clear a place for the menorah on the table itself, or because he does not bother to clear a place for it; therefore the cloth has not become a basis for the menorah, and it has not been forbidden in tiltul:
- **séif 10, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « שֶׁכֵּיוָן שֶׁהַקִּנָּה עֲשׂוּיָה לַתַּרְנְגוֹלִין — אֵין זֶה כְּשׁוֹכֵחַ אֶלָּא כְּמַנִּיחַ מִדַּעַת. וְכֵן כָּל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → since, as the nest is made for hens, this is not like forgetfulness but like placing it knowingly. And likewise in every similar case:
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְאִם עָשָׂה כֵּן לְטוֹבַת חֲבֵרוֹ … וְאָסוּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ כָּל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ. וְכֵן כָּל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → And if he did so for his fellow's benefit — since presumably his fellow is pleased with this thing that he did for his benefit — for example, Reuven took an earthenware vessel of Shimon and set it, while it was still day, under the lamp in Shimon's house so that the lamp would fall into it and the house would not burn, and the lamp fell into it before bein hashemashos and remained in it until after bein hashemashos — the vessel has become a basis for the lamp, and it is forbidden to move it all of Shabbos. And likewise in every similar case:

### Siman 310

- **séif 2, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee mot a mot sur l'hebreu (« ce qui est mis bamuktzeh » rendu « placed in the drying place (muktzeh) »)) — « אֲפִלּוּ אִם הוּא עוֹמֵד לִסְחוֹרָה … מֻתָּר לֶאֱכוֹל מֵהֶם בְּשַׁבָּת. וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם דָּחָה אוֹתָם בְּיָדַיִם … קֹדֶם גְּמַר בִּשּׁוּלָם. וְכֵן כָּל מִינֵי פֵּרוֹת … קֹדֶם שֶׁיִּתְיַבְּשׁוּ. + לְפִי שֶׁהֵן מַסְרִיחוֹת … עַד שֶׁיִּהְיוּ רְאוּיִין לְגַמְרֵי + וְאֵינָן דּוֹמִין לִשְׁאָר דְּבָרִים … הֻקְצָה לְכָל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ » → …even if it stands for trade, such as dates, almonds and other fruits that stand to be taken elsewhere for trade — it is permitted to eat from them on Shabbos. And even if he actively set them aside (dachah osam beyadayim) from eating them on Shabbos, such as wheat that he sowed in the ground — it is permitted … if they have not yet taken root. Likewise eggs placed under a hen to raise chicks — it is permitted to move them. And likewise dates picked before ripening and gathered into baskets, where they ripen by themselves — it is permitted to eat from them before their ripening is complete. And likewise all kinds of fruits placed in the drying place (muktzeh) to dry — permitted before they dry. [Except for …] because they become malodorous in the meantime and are not fit for eating once they have stayed there a little …; therefore they are forbidden on Shabbos on account of muktzeh. Needless to say if they are entirely unfit … like stones and earth, and even designation (hazmanah) while it was still day does not avail … But even if they are fit-and-not-fit … they are nevertheless forbidden …: since he actively set them aside, they do not leave their state of being set aside (dechiyah) until they are entirely fit. And they are not like other things he actively set aside, which are permitted … since he knew they would become malodorous, he set his mind entirely away from them … And although now, on Shabbos, he changes his mind to eat them — they do not become prepared (muchanim) thereby … for anything set aside by a person's intent at bein hashemashos is set aside for the whole of Shabbos:
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אִם הֵם רְאוּיִין וְאֵינָן רְאוּיִין … לְפִי שֶׁהַכֹּל מוּכָן אֶצְלוֹ, שֶׁהַנָּכְרִי אֵינוֹ מַקְצֶה כְּלוּם … מוּכָן לַכֹּל + בַּמֶּה דְּבָרִים אֲמוּרִים? בְּדָבָר תָּלוּשׁ … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן שכ״ה + וְדָבָר שֶׁהָיָה מְחֻסָּר צִידָה … אֵין זֶה מִן הַמּוּכָן » → …are permitted even if they are fit-and-not-fit, for there is no law of muktzeh regarding [items] of a non-Jew, because everything is prepared with him: the non-Jew does not set anything aside and his mind is on everything; and since it is prepared for one person, it is prepared for everyone. Of what does this speak? Of a detached thing; but something attached that was detached on Shabbos — even if it belongs to a non-Jew who detached it for himself, and even if the non-Jew had in mind while it was still day to detach these fruits on Shabbos, for example if he heard the non-Jew say on Erev Shabbos “Tomorrow I will detach these fruits,” and even if they are fruits whose ripening is complete, for which preparation (hachanah) is effective even while still attached, as will be explained in siman 318 — they are nevertheless forbidden in eating and in tiltul on account of the decree of fallen fruits (peiros ha-noshrin) that will be explained in siman 322, as will be explained in siman 325. And a thing that still required trapping (tzeidah) while it was still day, and the non-Jew trapped it on Shabbos — even if he had in mind to trap it on Shabbos, his intent avails nothing, for the matter does not depend on him; and it is forbidden on Shabbos on account of muktzeh, for since it was not in the possession of any person at all, it is not among what is prepared:
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « כְּגוֹן דָּבָר שֶׁהָיָה מֻקְצֶה … וְנַעֲשָׂה רָאוּי + דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁהָאָדָם גָּרַם לוֹ הָאִסּוּר בְּיָדַיִם, כְּגוֹן נֵר … הֻקְצָה לְכָל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ » → …and on Shabbos the thing causing its prohibition went away — for example, an object that was muktzeh at bein hashemashos on account of a prohibition, that is, because of some prohibition it was forbidden to eat and was not fit for anything at bein hashemashos, so that it was muktzeh and forbidden in tiltul, and on Shabbos the prohibition departed from it and it became fit — … Of what does this speak? When a person actively set it aside, that is, the person actively caused its prohibition — for example, a lamp that he lit while it was still day and that went out after bein hashemashos, and oil remained in it: this oil was set aside by human action from being fit on Shabbos, for when he lit it, it became forbidden to make use of it on Shabbos on account of the prohibition of extinguishing. Therefore it is forbidden to make use of it even after the lamp has gone out and the prohibition has departed: since at bein hashemashos, which is the beginning of the entry of Shabbos, it was muktzeh by the intent of the person, who set it aside by his intent when he caused its prohibition — it is set aside for the whole of Shabbos:
- **séif 5, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (« כשהכניסן ליבשן » rendu litteralement « when he brought them in to dry them »)) — « שֶׁהוּא אָסוּר בְּטִלְטוּל כָּל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ אִם נִדְחָה בִּידֵי אָדָם … הֻקְצוּ לְכָל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ » → …— it is forbidden in tiltul throughout all of Shabbos if it was set aside by human action from being fit at bein hashemashos; for example, dried figs and raisins that were not fit at bein hashemashos and on Shabbos dried completely and became fit: since at bein hashemashos they were muktzeh by the intent of the person, who set them aside by his intent when he brought them in to dry them — they are set aside for the whole of Shabbos:
- **séif 6, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « כְּגוֹן דָּבָר שֶׁהָיָה מֻקְצֶה מֵחֲמַת אִסּוּר הַבָּא מֵאֵלָיו … וְכֵן כָּל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → For example, an object that was muktzeh on account of a prohibition that came about by itself and not through human causation, and after nightfall the prohibition departed — there is no longer any muktzeh in it, even though when bein hashemashos arrived he removed his mind from it entirely and did not expect at all that the prohibition would depart on Shabbos. For example, live animals, which are muktzeh on Shabbos on account of the prohibition of slaughter, and he slaughtered them on Shabbos for a sick person who fell ill that day — it is permitted for a healthy person to eat from them raw, even though he removed his mind from them when bein hashemashos arrived on account of the prohibition of slaughter, since it is a prohibition that comes by itself and not through human causation. And likewise in every similar case:
- **séif 7, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱסַר בַּאֲכִילָה אוֹ בְּטִלְטוּל, בֵּין שֶׁנִּתְקַלְקֵל מֵאֵלָיו בֵּין שֶׁנִּדְחָה בִּידֵי אָדָם, וְאַחַר כָּךְ חָזַר + וְאֵין אוֹמְרִים מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁהֻקְצָה … בֵּין בְּאִסּוּר טִלְטוּל » → …became spoiled in such a way that it became forbidden in eating or in tiltul — whether it became spoiled by itself or was set aside by human action — and afterward was restored again — has returned to its permitted state. And we do not say: since it was set aside and became forbidden already for part of Shabbos, it is set aside for the entire remainder of Shabbos; for we say this only when it was set aside at the beginning of the entry of Shabbos, which is bein hashemashos, for then it is set aside for a whole day; but when it was not set aside until afterward, so that there is not a whole day here — it is not set aside for the remainder of the day, for there is no muktzeh for half a Shabbos, whether regarding the prohibition of eating or the prohibition of tiltul:
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « וַהֲרֵי הֵם רְאוּיִים לַאֲכִילָה, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא יָדְעוּ הַבְּעָלִים … + שֶׁמִּתְּחִלָּה כְּשֶׁהִקְצוּ אוֹתָן … וְהוּכְנוּ מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם » → …and were fit for eating — even though the owners did not know at all at that time that they had dried, and afterward it became known to them that at bein hashemashos they were already dry — are permitted in eating; for from the outset, when they set them aside and removed their minds from them, they set them aside only until they would dry, and they have dried and become prepared while it was still day:
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁהֵם רְאוּיִים וְאֵינָן רְאוּיִין, שֶׁיֵּשׁ אֲנָשִׁים … + מֻתָּר לֶאֱכוֹל מֵהֶן בְּשַׁבָּת, שֶׁכֵּיוָן שֶׁגִּלָּה דַעְתּוֹ … כְּמַזְמִין אֶת הָאֶבֶן לַאֲכִילָה » → …such that they are fit-and-not-fit — some people eat them and some do not — … it is permitted to eat from them on Shabbos: since he revealed his intent that they are fit for him, they have left the category of muktzeh. And if not — it is forbidden: since he did not reveal his intent, they remain in their state of being set aside, in which they were muktzeh from the outset, when they were not fit for any person. But if he designated for eating dried figs and raisins that are not fit at all — his designation avails nothing, for this is like designating a stone for eating:
- **séif 10, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אֲפִלּוּ אִם דַּעְתּוֹ לְנַעֲרוֹ … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן ר[ס]״ה וְרס״ו + שֶׁבְּזֶה אֵינוֹ אוֹסֵר … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן שי״א » → …even if he intends to shake it out afterward, because this renders the utensil forbidden in tiltul as long as the muktzeh is inside it, and he thus nullifies a utensil from its readiness (mevatel kli mei-heichano); and it is forbidden to nullify a utensil from its readiness even temporarily, except in a case of great loss, as was explained in simanim 265 and 266. … for by this he does not render the utensil forbidden in tiltul. Provided only that the utensil does not touch the muktzeh: for although muktzeh is permitted to be merely touched, even with one's hands, as long as one does not move it, as was explained in siman 308, nevertheless here, where placing the utensil on the muktzeh is for the sake of the muktzeh — even touching by means of the utensil is forbidden. And some dispute this and say that the Sages cautioned that the utensil should not touch the muktzeh only when the muktzeh is something that moves through the touch of the utensil, such as an egg and the like, so that he ends up moving it by means of the utensil; and they permitted moving by means of another object only for the sake of a permitted thing, as will be explained in siman 311:
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (« מלטלטלם » rendu litteralement « from moving them »)) — « שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה הַהֶתֵּר בָּסִיס לְהָאִסּוּר … אַף עַל פִּי כֵן + כְּגוֹן מִטָּה שֶׁהִנִּיחַ עָלֶיהָ מָעוֹת … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן רס״ה וְרס״ו » → …— such that the permitted object became a basis (basis) to the forbidden one and became forbidden in tiltul like it, as was explained in siman 309 — and after nightfall the forbidden object was taken off the permitted one: it is nevertheless forbidden to move it throughout all of Shabbos. For example, a bed on which he placed coins while it was still day with the intent that they remain there on Shabbos, and after nightfall the coins were taken off it by a non-Jew or a minor — it is forbidden to move it throughout all of Shabbos: since it was set aside at bein hashemashos by the intent of the person, who made it a basis to a forbidden thing — it is set aside for the whole of Shabbos. But if the coins were not on it at bein hashemashos, but afterward a non-Jew or a minor placed them on it — even if they placed them with the consent of the Jew, so that it was set aside by his intent from moving them as long as the coins are on it — it is nevertheless permitted to move it if the coins were taken off it, since it was not muktzeh at bein hashemashos. But while the coins are on it, it is forbidden to move it, on account of the prohibition of moving the coins that are on it. But it is permitted to tilt it and shake it and make the coins fall off it, and to move it afterward to wherever he wishes. And if he needs its place — he moves it with them on it, as was explained in siman 309 regarding a thing that did not become a basis to a forbidden thing; for this one too did not become a basis …, since the forbidden thing was not on it at bein hashemashos, as was explained in simanim 265 and 266:
- **séif 12, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (la glose FR « par crainte de dommage / objet de valeur réservé » n'est pas reprise ; rendu « because of financial loss »)) — « אוֹ אֲפִלּוּ יִחֲדָהּ לְמָעוֹת … וְסִלְּקוֹ מִן הַיִּחוּד » → …or even if he designated it for coins but has not yet ever placed coins on it, for by thought alone it does not become forbidden. But if he designated it for coins and performed an act with it, placing coins on it once, even on a weekday — it is forbidden to move it on Shabbos even for the sake of its use or its place. And it is not like other utensils designated for forbidden work, which it is permitted to move for the sake of their use or their place, because people are not particular about them against using them for another, permitted use; but this bed, designated for coins — a person is particular about it and designates a place for it, so as not to move it for another use, and it is thus similar to muktzeh because of financial loss (muktzeh mei-chamas chesron kis), which it is forbidden to move even for the sake of its use or its place, as was explained in siman 308. And some dispute this and permit moving it for the sake of its use or its place, like other utensils designated for forbidden work. And one should be stringent in accordance with the first opinion. Therefore one should not move a coin-purse even though he removed the coins from it while it was still day, unless he performed an act with it, that he opened it from below and removed it from its designation:
- **séif 13, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אִם הוֹצִיא הַמָּעוֹת מִשָּׁם — יוּכַל לִלְבּוֹשׁ הַבֶּגֶד, שֶׁהַכִּיס בָּטֵל אֶצְלוֹ … (וְצָרִיךְ עִיּוּן דְּאִם כֵּן לִשְׁתְּרֵי לְגַמְרֵי) » → …if he removed the coins from there, he may wear the garment, for the purse is nullified relative to it. And even in the purse itself it is permitted to put something inside it or to take from it, for its being set aside is only because a person is particular about it against using it for another use, and here we see that he is not particular about it (and this requires examination: if so, it should be permitted entirely): [remplace « — is permitted. », absent de la source]
- **séif 14, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן ש״א וְש״ג, אֲבָל מֻתָּר לְטַלְטֵל אֶת הַבֶּגֶד … שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה הַכִּיס בָּסִיס לָהֶם » → …as was explained in simanim 301 and 303; but it is permitted to move the garment. And even if he placed the coins in the purse with the intent that they remain there also on Shabbos, so that the purse became a basis for them — the garment nevertheless did not become a basis for them, since the coins are not on the main body of the garment; and it is the permitted thing that he moves with his hands, while the forbidden thing moves along with it by itself, through indirect moving (tiltul min ha-tzad), in which there is no prohibition when he is not moving for the sake of the forbidden thing but for the sake of the permitted thing, as will be explained in siman 311. But it is forbidden to put his hand into the purse if he placed the coins in it with the intent that they remain in it also on Shabbos, for the purse became a basis for them:
- **séif 15, EN** (traduite de l'hebreu en suivant la page FR ; deux ecarts deliberes a la FR : « תלוי לארכו » rendu « hung along its length » (la FR dit « cousue dans sa longueur ») et « והבגד לכיס הקשור ותלוי בו » rendu litteralement (la FR dit « le vêtement est lié à la bourse »)) — « אֲבָל אִם כֻּלּוֹ תָּלוּי לְאָרְכּוֹ בַּבֶּגֶד … עַל דֶּרֶךְ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר » → …but if it is entirely hung along its length on the garment — the whole garment becomes a basis for the coins in the purse, like the purse itself, for it is all one. And even if he placed the coins in the purse with the intent of taking them out of it before Shabbos and forgot …, so that not even the purse itself became a basis for them — nevertheless, if it is possible to shake the coins out of the purse, he must shake them out first, and afterward he may move the garment to any place he wishes, just as if the coins had been on the main body of the garment …, as was explained in siman 309 regarding coins on a cushion. And even with a purse that hangs entirely — they permitted moving the garment only when it is sewn to the garment (for then it is nullified relative to the garment …). But if a purse full of coins is tied to the garment, since the purse is a utensil in its own right — (it is not nullified relative to the garment, and therefore) it is forbidden to move (the whole) garment if he placed the coins in the purse with the intent that they remain there also on Shabbos (for the purse became a basis for them, and the garment for the purse that is tied to it and hangs on it). And even if the coins were taken from the purse (or the purse from the garment) after nightfall — it is forbidden to move (the whole) garment throughout all of Shabbos (since it became a basis at bein hashemashos). And if he placed them with the intent of taking them before Shabbos and forgot … — he must shake the coins out of the purse if it is possible …, and afterward he may move the garment … if he does not intend [to move it] for the sake of the coins, in order to put them away, as was explained in siman 309. And likewise, if there are coins in a box (teivah) inside the table — it is forbidden to move the table except in the manner that was explained, unless there is on the table, while it was still day, something permitted in tiltul, for then the table became at bein hashemashos a basis to issur and heter, and it is permitted to move it in the manner that will be explained:
- **séif 16, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אֲבָל אִם דָּבָר הָאָסוּר חָשׁוּב יוֹתֵר … עַיֵּן שָׁם כָּל פְּרָטֵי דִּין זֶה » → …; but if the forbidden item is more important than the permitted item — the heter is nullified relative to the issur, and it is forbidden even to shake off the issur, as if it were a basis to the issur alone, where it is forbidden even to shake off, as was explained there. And if the permitted item is not important to him because of his great wealth, even though it is important to everyone — it is not called important, for everything follows the owner, as was explained in siman 308; see there all the details of this law:
- **séif 17, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « וְאַחַר כָּךְ יְטַלְטֵל הַכְּלִי עִם הַהֶתֵּר … וְיִטּוֹל הַמַּחְתָּה בִּלְבַדָּהּ » → …and afterward move the utensil with the heter to the place he wishes. And if it is impossible to shake off the issur alone without the heter — he shakes off the heter too along with it, if there is no loss in shaking it off, and afterward he takes the heter alone and returns it to the utensil if he wishes, as was explained there. And if it is in such a way that, if he shakes off the heter too, he will not afterward be able to take the heter alone — for example, a fire-pan (machtah) on which there is, from while it was still day, ash that is permitted to move because it is fit to cover spittle or excrement with it, and there are also on it pieces of wood or coals that are forbidden in tiltul, and he needs to move the ash to another place to cover with it, and if he shakes it off together with the pieces and the coals he will not afterward be able to take the ash alone with his hands, for example if they are small pieces or small coals … — it is permitted to move the fire-pan as it is, with the ash, the pieces and the coals, to the place he wishes. Of what does this speak? When he needs the ash; but if he needs only the fire-pan itself — he should not move it as it is, but should shake off the ash, the pieces and the coals in their place, since there is no loss in shaking them off, and take the fire-pan alone:
- **séif 18, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (sans le crochet explicatif « [d'interdit seul] » de la FR)) — « וְאַף אִם מִשֶּׁחָשֵׁכָה נִטַּל הַהֶתֵּר מִשָּׁם … נֶאֱסַר לְכָל הַשַּׁבָּת כֻּלָּהּ » → …; and even if after nightfall the heter was taken from there — the utensil did not become a basis then, since it was not a basis at bein hashemashos, as was explained above. But if at bein hashemashos the issur alone was on it, and after nightfall the heter too was placed on it — this avails nothing: since at bein hashemashos it was a basis to the issur alone, it became forbidden for the whole of Shabbos:
- **séif 19, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִמָּן תצ״ח. וְאִם נִתְעָרֵב אֵפֶר … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר שָׁם » → …, as will be explained in siman 498. And if ash from a fire kindled on Shabbos became mixed with ash of the previous day — it is nullified by the majority, if it had never yet been recognizable on its own, as will be explained there:
- **séif 20, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « עַל פִּי הַתְּנָאִים שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֲרוּ » → …, according to the conditions that have been explained:

### Siman 311

- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (« שלא לצרך גופו או מקומו » rendu litteralement, la glose FR « tiltoul tinok » non reprise)) — « שֶׁהִתִּירוּ חֲכָמִים אִסּוּר טִלְטוּל … שֶׁלֹּא יִשָּׂרֵף ; דְּהַיְנוּ אִם יֵשׁ לוֹ דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר בְּטִלְטוּל שֶׁלֹּא לְצֹרֶךְ גּוּפוֹ אוֹ מְקוֹמוֹ … וּמְטַלְטֵל שְׁנֵיהֶם כְּאֶחָד ; וְאִם אֵין לוֹ דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר … לְהַצִּילוֹ מִן הַדְּלֵקָה » → for the Sages permitted the prohibition of tiltul so that one should not come to the grave prohibition of extinguishing the fire, since he is distraught over his dead lest it be burned. … That is: if one has something that is permitted to be moved not for the sake of its use or its place — such as an infant, or a kikar (loaf of bread), or other foods, or any vessel whose work is for a permitted use — one moves it by its means; that is, one places the permitted object upon the mes or beside it and moves both of them together. And if one has nothing permitted to be moved: if the mes is lying on a bed and one has another bed — one turns him over from this bed to that bed, and from that one to this one, until one brings him to a place protected from the fire; for this is tiltul min ha-tzad, and the bed does not become a basis for the mes, since one has no need of it, for all one needs is to lay him on the ground. And if one has no other bed — one carries him on this bed; or even if he is not lying on a bed, one moves him with a full tiltul with one's hands to save him from the fire.
- **séif 2, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (remplace l'ancienne glose EN fautive « a negative commandment of rabbinic origin »)) — « שֶׁבַּתּוֹרָה, שֶׁהִיא "לֹא תָסוּר … וגו׳" … וְאִסּוּר הַכַּרְמְלִית גַּם כֵּן אֵינוֹ אֶלָּא מִדִּבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים ; וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן, אִם יֵשׁ לוֹ דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר … מִלִּדְחוֹת שְׁנֵי אִסּוּרִים » → lest it be burned; for great is kevod ha-beriyos (human dignity), which overrides a negative commandment of the Torah, namely “You shall not turn aside from the word that they tell you, etc.” — for by this commandment we were commanded to heed all the words of the Soferim — and the prohibition of karmelis too is only of the words of the Soferim. And nevertheless, if one has something permitted to be moved — one must carry it out together with him, in order to lighten the prohibition of tiltul. For although the karmelis prohibition is set aside because of kevod ha-beriyos, nevertheless the prohibition of tiltul, since it can be remedied by moving a permitted object — one must remedy it in whatever way possible. And although one adds to the karmelis prohibition by carrying out with him the permitted object as well — this is of no consequence, for the karmelis prohibition has already been set aside entirely in carrying out the mes. But the prohibition of tiltul, since it can be remedied, we are not to set it aside; for it is better to add within one prohibition than to set aside two prohibitions.
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « שֶׁלֹּא הִתִּירוּ טִלְטוּל מִן הַצַּד אֶלָּא כְּשֶׁמְּטַלְטֵל לְצֹרֶךְ דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר … אֶלָּא בִּדְלֵקָה בִּלְבַד הִתִּירוּהוּ, מִשּׁוּם שֶׁלֹּא יָבֹא לְכַבּוֹת » → for they permitted tiltul min ha-tzad only when one moves for the sake of a permitted thing, as will be explained, whereas here one moves for the sake of the mes, which is forbidden to be moved. And on account of the honor of the mes they permitted only the prohibition of carrying out into a karmelis, which cannot be remedied at all when there is need for it; but the prohibition of tiltul, since it can be remedied by moving a permitted object together with him — they did not permit it in any manner, even if one has no permitted object; only in a fire did they permit it, so that one should not come to extinguish.
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « עַל יְדֵי דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר, אִם אֶפְשָׁר לַעֲשׂוֹת לוֹ תַּקָּנָה … כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר. אֲבָל אִם אִי אֶפְשָׁר בְּכָךְ … וְכֵן כָּל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → by means of a permitted object, if a remedy can be made for it by spreading mats for shade, as will be explained. But if that is not possible, and likewise a mes lying in the house that is close to putrefying, and one has no cool place in the same domain to remove him to — some permit carrying him out into a karmelis by means of a permitted object, on account of bizayon ha-mes, lest he putrefy. But if he has already putrefied in the house and one wishes to carry him out in order to remove his odor from the members of the household — if they have another place to go out to, one does not carry him out; rather one leaves him in his place and they go out. And if not — it is permitted to carry him out, for great is kevod ha-beriyos. Likewise if one wishes to carry him out for the sake of his honor, so that he not be disgraced among the living — it is permitted to carry him out if they have no other place to go out to. Likewise if he lies in some other disgrace — for example, he was on a ship and non-Jews were gathering there, which is something of a disgrace to him — it is permitted to bring him through a karmelis to the house by means of a permitted object. And so in every similar case.
- **séif 5, EN** (traduite de l hebreu (la phrase FR « n'est interdit par la Torah qu'au titre des paroles des Sopherim » est ambigue) ; remplace l'ancienne glose EN « for the prohibition there is essentially Torah-based ») — « וַאֲפִלּוּ לְהָאוֹמְרִים שֶׁהוֹצָאַת הַמֵּת אֵינָהּ אֲסוּרָה מִן הַתּוֹרָה … מַה שֶּׁאֵין כֵּן הַכַּרְמְלִית עִקָּרָהּ מִדִּבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים » → And even according to those who say that carrying out the mes is not forbidden by the Torah but only by the words of the Soferim, because it is a melachah she'einah tzerichah legufah (a labor not needed for its own sake), as was explained in siman 278 — they did not permit it on account of kevod ha-beriyos, since its essence is from the Torah, that is, if it were needed for its own sake; which is not so of karmelis, whose essence is from the words of the Soferim.
- **séif 6, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אַף שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה הַחִלּוּל עַל יְדֵי נָכְרִי, מַה שֶּׁאֵין כֵּן הַכַּרְמְלִית הַכֹּל יוֹדְעִין שֶׁאֵינָהּ בִּכְלַל מְלָאכוֹת וְעִקָּרָהּ מִדִּבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים » → even though the desecration was done by a non-Jew; which is not so of karmelis — everyone knows that it is not among the melachos and that its essence is from the words of the Soferim.
- **séif 7, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אֲבָל שְׁאָר דְּבָרִים הָאֲסוּרִים בְּטִלְטוּל — אָסוּר לְטַלְטְלָם … לֹא הִתִּירוּ אֶלָּא בְּמֵת בִּלְבַד » → but other things forbidden to be moved — it is forbidden to move them by means of a permitted object, even if one is not moving for the sake of the forbidden thing but for the sake of a permitted thing. For they permitted tiltul by means of another object, for the sake of a permitted thing, only when one moves the permitted item with one's hands and the forbidden item is moved along with it; but when one moves the forbidden item with one's hands and the permitted item is moved along with it — they permitted it only for the mes alone.
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אֲבָל לֹא לְצֹרֶךְ כֹּהֲנִים שֶׁיִּשָּׁאֲרוּ בְּבָתֵּיהֶם, אוֹ לְצֹרֶךְ דָּבָר אַחֵר, אֲפִלּוּ הוּא דְבַר מִצְוָה ; אֲפִילוּ שֶׁלֹּא עַל יְדֵי דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר, שֶׁכָּל שְׁבוּת דִּשְׁבוּת … אֲפִלּוּ שֶׁלֹּא עַל יְדֵי דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר » → — but not for the sake of Kohanim, so that they may remain in their houses, or for the sake of some other matter, even if it is a matter of mitzvah. … even not by means of a permitted object, for any shevus di-shevus is permitted in a case of mitzvah. And needless to say, wherever it is permitted to move him by means of a permitted object for the sake of his honor, it is permitted to move him by means of a non-Jew even not by means of a permitted object.
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אוֹ לַדָּבָר שֶׁהַמֵּת מֻנָּח עָלָיו, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁלֹּא אָסְרוּ טִלְטוּל מִן הַצַּד … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״ט בְּשׁוֹכֵחַ אֶבֶן עַל פִּי הֶחָבִית » → or the thing on which the mes is lying; because they forbade tiltul min ha-tzad only when one moves for the sake of the forbidden thing, that is, for the sake of the mes, which is forbidden to be moved; but here one is not moving for the sake of the mes but for the sake of another, permitted thing. And if one has no other bed — it is permitted to move him on this bed, since it is impossible to shake it so as to drop him off it onto the ground, for then one could no longer move him by tiltul min ha-tzad; and whatever cannot be shaken off — it is permitted to move it in its usual manner when one is not moving it for the sake of the forbidden thing, as was explained in siman 309 regarding one who forgets a stone on the mouth of a barrel.
- **séif 10, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (remplace « But not when he is still wearing his ordinary garment. »)) — « אֲבָל מֵת שֶׁהוּא לָבוּשׁ בְּבִגְדּוֹ … וּלְהַלְבִּישׁוֹ תַּכְרִיכִים » → But a mes who is clothed in the garment he wore during his lifetime — there is no need to place anything upon him, for this garment is permitted to be moved, since it is not bittel to him, because they are going to strip it off him and clothe him in tachrichim.
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (remplace le resume entre parentheses)) — « אוֹ שֶׁלֹּא רָצוּ לַהֲזִיזוֹ מִמְּקוֹמוֹ ; כֵּיצַד? בָּאִים שְׁנֵי בְּנֵי אָדָם … וּכְשֶׁעָמַד זְקָפָהּ וְהָלַךְ לוֹ » → or they did not wish to move him from his place … How? Two people come and sit on the ground on his two sides. When they are hot from below, from the heat of the ground that the sun has heated — this one brings his bed and sits on it, and that one brings his bed and sits on it. When they are hot from above — this one brings a mat and spreads it over himself for shade, and that one brings a mat and spreads it over himself. This one stands his bed upright, removes it from there, slips away and goes off, and that one stands his bed upright and removes it, slips away and goes off — and a partition is found to have been made of itself: for the mat of this one and the mat of that one have their tops adjoining one another, and their two ends on the ground on the two sides of the mes. For they permitted making an ohel arai for the sake of the distress of the living and not for the sake of the mes; therefore they required that they first sit until they become hot from below, and then bring beds to sit on — although the beds are of no use at all for making the tent — so that it should appear that the spreading of the mats is not in order to make a tent over the mes by joining the two of them, but in order that each one spread over himself to protect against the sun, for also when they were hot from below each one brought his bed and they sat on them. And for this reason they must stand their beds upright and remove them from there and leave the spread mats standing, so that here too it should appear that the spreading of the mats was not for the sake of the mes but to protect them from the sun: for when he was hot from below he brought a bed and sat on it, and when he got up he stood it upright and went off.
- **séif 12, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ עַל יְדֵי דָּבָר הַמֻּתָּר אָסוּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ כְּדֵי לְרָחֳצוֹ … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״א » → And even by means of a permitted object it is forbidden to move him in order to wash him, unless he was soiled with mud and excrement to the point that he is repulsive in the eyes of those who see him; for they permitted moving the mes by means of a permitted object only to save him from disgrace — such as when he lies in the sun, lest he putrefy, or some other disgrace, as was explained above. Therefore one must protest against the practice that has spread of lifting the mes off the cushions by means of a permitted object even where there is no concern that he may putrefy. And even if there is concern that he will putrefy from the heat of the cushions — one slides the cushions out from under him, and one does not move him. And it is permitted to sprinkle vinegar on the garments of the mes so that he not putrefy by the conclusion of Shabbos, and they will be able to attend to his taharah; and there is no issue of libbun (whitening) here, for vinegar does not whiten; and the decree lest one wring out, too, is decreed only for a whitening liquid, as was explained in siman 301.
- **séif 13, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (remplace la parenthese « though nowadays the custom has spread to do so, claiming danger »)) — « אוֹ קְצָתוֹ, שֶׁהֲרֵי אָסוּר לְהָזִיז אֵבֶר ; וְעַכְשָׁו נָהֲגוּ לְהַעֲצִים עֵינָיו … וְאֵין לִמְחוֹת בְּיָדָם » → or part of it, for it is forbidden to move a limb. … And nowadays the custom has spread to close his eyes on Shabbos and to move a limb so that it not become distorted, and they say that there is danger in the matter; and one should not protest against them.
- **séif 14, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אוֹ זֶה בָּזֶה אוֹ זֶה עַל זֶה … בְּסִימָן ש״ט, וּבִזְמַן שֶׁמְּטַלְטֵל … בְּשׁוּם עִנְיָן ; וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם מְטַלְטֵל הַהֶתֵּר בְּיָדָיו … וְכֵן הַדִּין בְּעָפָר שֶׁיִּחֲדוֹ לְכָךְ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר » → or one in the other, or one on the other, and it is in a manner that the permitted one has not become a basis for the forbidden one, as was explained in siman 309, and when one of them is moved the second is moved along with it: … they did not permit it in any manner, except for the mes alone. And even if one moves the permitted one with one's hands and the forbidden one is moved along with it, if in this tiltul one intends it for the sake of the forbidden one — needless to say if one moves it for the sake of the forbidden item itself, to protect it from some damage or loss, or to put it away, or for some other purpose, but even if one moves it because one needs the forbidden item to make some permitted use of it, such as to sit or lie on it, which is permitted, as was explained in siman 308, or such as a lamp, to use its light in another place, and anything similar — since it is being moved for the sake of the forbidden item, it is forbidden. But if one has no need at all of the forbidden item itself, and one moves it by means of the permitted one only for the sake of a permitted thing, such as to clear its place — this is the tiltul min ha-tzad that the Sages permitted, in the manner explained in siman 309 regarding a stone on the mouth of a barrel and coins on a cushion. And needless to say if one has no intention at all of moving the forbidden item … such as a detached radish buried in the ground with some of its leaves exposed — one takes it by them and pulls it out, even though in taking it one moves earth from its place. This is provided that it has not taken root, for then there is no issue of tolesh (uprooting), even if one intended it for planting when one buried it there, as was explained in siman 310. And even if it grew because of the moisture of the ground — that is of no consequence, so long as it has not taken root. And the radish has not become a basis for the earth on top of it, even if one had in mind that it should remain lying there the whole of Shabbos, because one's intention was not that the radish should serve the earth, but that the earth should serve the radish. And likewise in every similar case. And if none of its leaves are exposed — some permit inserting a spindle or a knife into the radish and pulling it out with them, and the earth on top of it shakes off by itself. And although at the start of lifting the radish one lifts the earth on it as well — that is of no consequence, since it is for the sake of a permitted thing. And some forbid inserting into the earth, because it looks like making a hole (guma). And as for the halachah, in matters of the words of the Soferim — follow the lenient view. But fruits buried in straw or chaff that are muktzeh for kindling — according to all it is permitted to insert a spindle or a knife into the fruits and take them out, for the prohibition of making a hole applies only to earth and the like, as will be explained in siman 506. And if one designated the straw and the chaff for burying the fruits in them — they have left the category of muktzeh and are permitted to be moved.
