And the same law applies to earth that one designated for this, as was explained.
- **séif 15, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (remplace la parenthese « e.g. straw on a bed »)) — « כְּגוֹן קַשׁ שֶׁהוּא מֻקְצֶה … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר שָׁם » → for example, straw, which is muktzeh because ordinarily it stands for kindling: if it was placed on the bed not for lying on, and on Shabbos one comes to lie on it — it is permitted to shake it with one's body so that it be fluffed up, soft and comfortable to lie on. And if one placed a cushion or a sheet on it while it was still day — it becomes as though one had sat or lain on it while it was still day, and it is thus prepared for sitting or lying; and from now on it has the status of a vessel, and it is permitted to move it with one's hands. Likewise if while it was still day one had in mind to sit or lie on it, as was explained in siman 308, with an item that is also usually designated for sitting or lying. And if one designated it for animal fodder while it was still day — it is permitted to move it in any manner. And all this is in places where straw ordinarily stands for kindling; but in places where it ordinarily stands for animal fodder or for lying on — it is permitted to move it in any manner, as was explained there.

### Siman 313

- **séif 1, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise), corrige l'imprécision relevée par l'arbitre) — « וְתוֹסֶפֶת אֹהֶל עֲרַאי מֻתֶּרֶת » → and a temporary addition to an ohel is permitted
- **séif 1, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise), elle corrige trois imprécisions) — « כְּלָל … לִפְקוֹק בּוֹ … וְתוֹסֶפֶת אֹהֶל עֲרַאי מֻתֶּרֶת » → « ne s'applique pas du tout le statut d'ustensile » · « pour boucher avec elle » · « un ajout provisoire à une tente (tossefet ohel arai) est permis »
- **séif 2, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage). Elle remplace l'ancienne phrase EN calquée sur le FR (« tied to the door… thin cord… gelustra »).) — « אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן תִּקְּנוֹ וְעָשָׂה בוֹ מַעֲשֶׂה… (שֶׁאָז נִכָּר… לְבַטְּלוֹ שָׁם לְעוֹלָם). וְאָז מֻתָּר… / וְגַם מֻתָּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ… / אֲבָל אִם הוּא בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁאָסוּר… כְּדֵין כְּלִי הַמְיֻחָד לִמְלֶאכֶת אִסּוּר: » → unless he prepared it and performed a deed upon it and made it ready for that, in such a way that its preparation demonstrates that it stands for that (…) And then it is permitted to lock with it even if he has never yet locked with it from while still day. / And it is also permitted to move it… / But if it is in a situation in which it is forbidden to lock with it on Shabbos… like the law of a kli designated for forbidden work:
- **séif 2, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle REMPLACE l'ancienne condition « attaché à la porte de jour… corde mince… guelustra », qui était la loi du néger et contredisait le texte.) — « אָסוּר לִנְעוֹל בּוֹ בְּשַׁבָּת, אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן תִּקְּנוֹ… וְאָז מֻתָּר… מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם. וְגַם מֻתָּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ… אֲבָל אִם הוּא בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁאָסוּר… כְּדֵין כְּלִי הַמְיֻחָד לִמְלֶאכֶת אִסּוּר: » → — il est interdit de fermer avec lui Shabbat, à moins qu'il ne l'ait aménagé, qu'il ait accompli sur lui un acte et l'ait préparé pour cela, de telle sorte que son aménagement atteste qu'il est destiné à cela (car alors il est reconnaissable qu'il ne l'annule pas là… pour l'y annuler à jamais). Et alors il est permis de fermer avec lui, même s'il n'a encore jamais fermé avec lui de jour. / Et il est aussi permis de le déplacer, même sans que ce soit pour l'usage de l'objet lui-même ou pour sa place… / Mais s'il est dans une situation où il est interdit de fermer avec lui Shabbat… comme la règle d'un ustensile destiné à un usage interdit :
- **séif 4, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « (אוֹ בַּמְּזוּזָה, שֶׁאַף שֶׁאֵין הַדָּבָר נִכָּר כָּל כָּךְ… הוֹאִיל וְהוּא דוֹמֶה לִכְלִי). וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם הַחֶבֶל הוּא אָרֹךְ… עַל יְדֵי הַחֶבֶל: » → (or to the doorpost, mezuzah — for although it is not so recognizable… since it resembles a kli). And even if the cord is long… since it is joined there from while still day by means of the cord:
- **séif 4, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle remplace la paraphrase inventée « puisque cette guelustra visible à sa tête atteste… ».) — « בִּבְרִיחַ הַדֶּלֶת (אוֹ בַּמְּזוּזָה, שֶׁאַף שֶׁאֵין הַדָּבָר נִכָּר כָּל כָּךְ… הוֹאִיל וְהוּא דוֹמֶה לִכְלִי). וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם הַחֶבֶל הוּא אָרֹךְ… עַל יְדֵי הַחֶבֶל: » → mais à la barre (beriah) de la porte (ou au montant, la mezouzah : car bien qu'il ne soit pas tellement reconnaissable qu'il est attaché là de jour… il n'apparaît néanmoins pas comme construisant Shabbat, puisqu'il ressemble à un ustensile). Et même si la corde est longue… puisqu'il est relié là de jour au moyen de la corde :
- **séif 5, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « (שֶׁכְּשֶׁהוּא קָשׁוּר יָפֶה בְּחֶבֶל חָזָק… וְשׁוּב אֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּבוֹנֶה בְּשַׁבָּת), וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם כֻּלּוֹ מֻנָּח בָּאָרֶץ כְּשֶׁשּׁוֹמְטוֹ מֵהַחוֹר שֶׁבָּאַסְקֻפָּה: » → (for when it is tied well with a strong cord, it is recognizable that it stands for the locking of a door… so it no longer appears as building on Shabbos), and even if it all lies on the ground when he draws it out of the hole in the threshold:
- **séif 5, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « (שֶׁכְּשֶׁהוּא קָשׁוּר יָפֶה בְּחֶבֶל חָזָק… וְשׁוּב אֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּבוֹנֶה בְּשַׁבָּת), וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם כֻּלּוֹ מֻנָּח בָּאָרֶץ כְּשֶׁשּׁוֹמְטוֹ מֵהַחוֹר שֶׁבָּאַסְקֻפָּה: » → (car lorsqu'il est bien attaché par une corde solide, il est reconnaissable qu'il sert à la fermeture d'une porte, bien qu'il ne soit pas attaché à la porte elle-même, et il n'apparaît plus comme construisant Shabbat), et même s'il repose tout entier à terre lorsqu'on le tire du trou du seuil :
- **séif 6, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בּוֹ עַל דֶּרֶךְ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר: » → and it is permitted to lock with it in the manner explained:
- **séif 6, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « אֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּבוֹנֶה, וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בּוֹ עַל דֶּרֶךְ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר: » → — il n'apparaît pas comme construisant, et il est permis de fermer avec lui de la manière qui a été expliquée :
- **séif 7, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « וְכֵן הַבְּרִיחַ שֶׁתּוֹחֲבִין קָצֶה הָאֶחָד… וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בּוֹ אַף שֶׁאֵינוֹ קָשׁוּר כְּלָל: » → And likewise the bolt (beriach)… if it has a ring in its middle… this is a handle for the bolt, and it is permitted to lock with it even though it is not tied at all:
- **séif 7, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « וְכֵן הַבְּרִיחַ שֶׁתּוֹחֲבִין קָצֶה הָאֶחָד… וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בּוֹ אַף שֶׁאֵינוֹ קָשׁוּר כְּלָל: » → Et de même la barre (beriah) dont on enfonce une extrémité dans le trou de la muraille d'un côté de la porte et l'autre extrémité dans le trou de l'autre côté : si elle a en son milieu un anneau… c'est là un manche pour la barre, et il est permis de fermer avec elle même si elle n'est pas attachée du tout :
- **séif 8, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « שֶׁגְּדָרָהּ בַּקּוֹצִים… אִם יֵשׁ צִיר… מֻתָּר… אֶלָּא שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לִזָּהֵר… אֲבָל אִם אֵין לָהֶן הֶכֵּר צִיר — אָסוּר… כָּל שֶׁאֵין בָּהֶן הֶכֵּר שֶׁהֵן דְּלָתוֹת: » → which he fenced with thorns… if there is a tsir in these doors… it is permitted… Only that one must be careful… But if they have no recognizable tsir — it is forbidden… so long as they have no sign that they are doors:
- **séif 8, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle remplace « qu'on bouche [par] de telles choses : », qui omettait toute la décision.) — « שֶׁגְּדָרָהּ בַּקּוֹצִים וַעֲשָׂאוּם כְּעֵין דֶּלֶת… מֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בָּהֶן בְּשַׁבָּת… אֶלָּא שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לִזָּהֵר… אֲבָל אִם אֵין לָהֶן הֶכֵּר צִיר — אָסוּר… כָּל שֶׁאֵין בָּהֶן הֶכֵּר שֶׁהֵן דְּלָתוֹת: » → une brèche… qu'on a clôturée d'épines dont on a fait une sorte de porte… si ces portes ont un tsir, ou même… dont la place est reconnaissable, il est permis de fermer avec elles Shabbat… Seulement, il faut prendre garde… que le tsir ne revienne pas dans son trou… / Mais si elles n'ont pas de marque de tsir — il est interdit… comme la construction d'une cloison fixe, tant qu'elles ne portent pas de marque qu'elles sont des portes :
- **séif 9, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּבוֹנֶה בִּנְעִילָה זוֹ, הוֹאִיל וְאַף כְּשֶׁהֵן פְּתוּחִין… שֶׁאֵינָן נִגְרָרוֹת בָּאָרֶץ: » → since he does not appear as building with this locking, given that even when they are open they are joined and fixed there well, so that they do not drag on the ground:
- **séif 9, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle remplace la raison inventée « ne descend pas et ne monte pas… porte ordinaire ».) — « מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּבוֹנֶה בִּנְעִילָה זוֹ, הוֹאִיל וְאַף כְּשֶׁהֵן פְּתוּחִין הֵן מְחֻבָּרִים וּקְבוּעִין שָׁם הֵיטֵב שֶׁאֵינָן נִגְרָרוֹת בָּאָרֶץ: » → car il n'apparaît pas comme construisant par cette fermeture, puisque même lorsqu'elles sont ouvertes, elles sont bien reliées et fixées là, de sorte qu'elles ne traînent pas au sol :
- **séif 11, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « וְיֵשׁ לְהָקֵל בְּדִבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים: » → And one may be lenient in matters of the words of the Sofrim:
- **séif 11, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « וְיֵשׁ לְהָקֵל בְּדִבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים: » → Et l'on peut être indulgent dans les paroles des Sofrim :
- **séif 13, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בָּהֶם אַף שֶׁשּׁוֹמְטִין… אֶלָּא שֶׁהוּא פֶּתַח הֶעָשׂוּי לִכְנִיסָה וִיצִיאָה תָּדִיר: » → and it is permitted to lock with them even though one removes it completely when opening, and even though they have no recognizable tsir, provided only that it is an opening made for regular entry and exit:
- **séif 13, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « וּמֻתָּר לִנְעוֹל בָּהֶם אַף שֶׁשּׁוֹמְטִין אוֹתָהּ לְגַמְרֵי כְּשֶׁפּוֹתְחִין, וְאַף שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם הֶכֵּר צִיר, אֶלָּא שֶׁהוּא פֶּתַח הֶעָשׂוּי לִכְנִיסָה וִיצִיאָה תָּדִיר: » → et il est permis de fermer avec elles bien qu'on l'enlève entièrement à l'ouverture, et bien qu'elles n'aient pas de marque de tsir, pourvu seulement que ce soit une ouverture destinée à l'entrée et à la sortie régulières :
- **séif 14, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « אֲבָל אִם יָצָא מִקְצָתוֹ — דּוֹחֲקוֹ… אֲבָל אִם יָצָא צִיר הָעֶלְיוֹן… כְּשֶׁהָעֶלְיוֹן הוּא בִּמְקוֹמוֹ: » → But if part of it came out — he pushes it until he returns it to its place… But if the upper tsir came out, even part of it — it is forbidden… when the upper one is in its place:
- **séif 14, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « אֲבָל אִם יָצָא מִקְצָתוֹ — דּוֹחֲקוֹ… אֲבָל אִם יָצָא צִיר הָעֶלְיוֹן, אֲפִלּוּ מִקְצָתוֹ — אָסוּר… כְּשֶׁהָעֶלְיוֹן הוּא בִּמְקוֹמוֹ: » → Mais s'il n'en est sorti qu'en partie — on le pousse jusqu'à le remettre à sa place… Mais si c'est le pivot supérieur qui est sorti, même en partie — il est interdit… l'inférieur tenant de lui-même dans son trou lorsque le supérieur est à sa place :
- **séif 17, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « הֲרֵי זֶה שְׁבוּת גָּמוּר… וּלְהָאוֹמְרִים… לְפִיכָךְ, אֵין צָרִיךְ לִמְחוֹת… יַחְמִיר לְעַצְמוֹ… לְהַזְהִיר… (שֶׁקּוֹרִין קוואטיר״ן)… » → this is a complete shevus, and not a shevus de-shevus… And according to those who say… / Therefore, one need not protest… every person should be stringent for himself… one must also warn the public… (which are called kvatirn)…
- **séif 17, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle remplace la glose fausse « shevout [rabbinique] pour une chose qui comporte… hattat [biblique] » et ajoute le second avis et la conclusion pratique.) — « הֲרֵי זֶה שְׁבוּת גָּמוּר, וְאֵינוֹ שְׁבוּת דִּשְׁבוּת… וּלְהָאוֹמְרִים… לְפִיכָךְ, אֵין צָרִיךְ לִמְחוֹת… אֲבָל כָּל אָדָם יַחְמִיר לְעַצְמוֹ… וְגַם צָרִיךְ לְהַזְהִיר… (שֶׁקּוֹרִין קוואטיר״ן)… » → c'est un shevout complet, et non un shevout de-shevout, que l'on a permis en cas de souffrance (siman 307). Et selon ceux qui disent… (siman 278) — … un shevout de-shevout… / C'est pourquoi il n'est pas nécessaire de protester contre ceux qui sont indulgents… Mais chacun sera rigoureux pour lui-même selon le premier avis, qui est l'essentiel. Et il faut aussi avertir le public… (qu'on appelle kvatirn)… un grave interdit :
- **séif 19, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « וְחַיָּב מִשּׁוּם בּוֹנֶה. וְהַשּׁוֹבֵר כְּלִי שָׁלֵם… / וְכֵן הַתּוֹקֵעַ הָעֵץ בְּתוֹךְ הַקַּרְדֹּם… כְּמוֹ הַסּוֹתֵר בִּנְיָן גָּמוּר: » → and he is liable on account of boneh. And one who breaks a whole kli… / And likewise one who fastens the wood into the axe… like one who demolishes a complete building:
- **séif 19, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « וְחַיָּב מִשּׁוּם בּוֹנֶה. וְהַשּׁוֹבֵר כְּלִי שָׁלֵם… וְכֵן הַתּוֹקֵעַ הָעֵץ בְּתוֹךְ הַקַּרְדֹּם… וְכָל הַמְפָרֵק עֵץ תָּקוּעַ — חַיָּב מִשּׁוּם סוֹתֵר… » → …et il est passible au titre de bonéh. Et celui qui brise un ustensile entier… passible au titre de sotéah. / Et de même, celui qui enfonce le bois dans la hache… c'est une toledah de bonéh… Et quiconque démonte un bois enfoncé est passible au titre de sotéah, comme celui qui démolit une construction véritable :
- **séif 21, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « מֻתָּר לְהַחֲזִירוֹ וּלְהַדְּקוֹ קְצָת… / וְיֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים שֶׁאֵין חִלּוּק… / אֲבָל כּוֹסוֹת הָעֲשׂוּיִים בַּחֲרִיצִים… / וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם, כִּסּוּיֵי כֵּלִים… (שֶׁקּוֹרִין שרוי״ף)… בְּסִימָן שי״ד: » → it is permitted to return it and tighten it somewhat… / And some say that there is no distinction… one should be stringent according to their words, unless… great need… / But cups made with grooves… forbidden according to all opinions… / Nevertheless, covers of kelim… (shroyf)… no prohibition at all…
- **séif 21, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise). Elle remplace « car ce n'est pas un montage solide » et rétablit la décision d'être rigoureux.) — « מֻתָּר לְהַחֲזִירוֹ וּלְהַדְּקוֹ קְצָת… וְיֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים שֶׁאֵין חִלּוּק… וְיֵשׁ לְהַחֲמִיר כְּדִבְרֵיהֶם… אֲבָל כּוֹסוֹת הָעֲשׂוּיִים בַּחֲרִיצִים… וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם, כִּסּוּיֵי כֵּלִים… (שֶׁקּוֹרִין שרוי״ף)… בְּסִימָן שי״ד: » → il est permis de la remettre et de la serrer un peu… car ce n'est pas du tout l'usage d'une coupe de la serrer si fortement qu'on en deviendrait passible au titre de bonéh… / Et certains disent qu'il n'y a pas de différence… Et il faut être rigoureux selon leurs paroles, sauf en cas de grand besoin… / Mais les coupes faites avec des rainures… interdit selon tous les avis… / Toutefois, les couvercles… (qu'on appelle shroyf)… ne comportent aucun interdit… comme cela sera expliqué au siman 314 :
- **séif 23, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁלֹּא יוּכַל שׁוּב לִטְּלוֹ מִשָּׁם — אָסוּר, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁמְּבַטֵּל כְּלִי מֵהֵיכָנוֹ. סַפְסָל שֶׁנִּשְׁמְטָה אַחַת מֵרַגְלָיו — נִתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״ח: » → in such a way that he will no longer be able to take it from there — it is forbidden, since he nullifies a kli from its readiness [for use]. / A bench one of whose legs has come off — has been explained in siman 308:
- **séif 23, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « בְּעִנְיָן שֶׁלֹּא יוּכַל שׁוּב לִטְּלוֹ מִשָּׁם — אָסוּר, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁמְּבַטֵּל כְּלִי מֵהֵיכָנוֹ. סַפְסָל שֶׁנִּשְׁמְטָה אַחַת מֵרַגְלָיו — נִתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״ח: » → Mais s'il l'y serre avec force, de sorte qu'il ne pourra plus l'ôter de là — c'est interdit, car il retire à un ustensile sa disponibilité (mevatel kli me-hekhano). / Un banc dont l'un des pieds s'est détaché — cela a été expliqué au siman 308 :
- **séif 24, EN** (traduite de l'hébreu (premier passage)) — « שֶׁכֵּיוָן שֶׁהוּא בָּא לְתַקֵּן הֶחָצֵר… אֲבָל בְּעִנְיָן אַחֵר — מֻתָּר… כֵּיוָן שֶׁאֵינוֹ מִתְכַּוֵּן לְהַשְׁווֹת פָּנֶיהָ: » → for since he comes to repair the courtyard — it appears as if he also intends to level its surface. But in another situation — it is permitted to spread in it even a thing that he nullifies there, since he does not intend to level its surface:
- **séif 24, FR** (traduite de l'hébreu (reprise)) — « שֶׁכֵּיוָן שֶׁהוּא בָּא לְתַקֵּן הֶחָצֵר… אֲבָל בְּעִנְיָן אַחֵר — מֻתָּר לִזְרוֹת בָּהּ אֲפִלּוּ דָּבָר שֶׁמְּבַטְּלוֹ שָׁם… » → puisqu'il vient réparer la cour, cela apparaît comme s'il entendait aussi en égaliser la surface. Mais dans un autre cas — il est permis d'y répandre même une chose qu'il y annule, puisqu'il n'entend pas en égaliser la surface :

### Siman 323

- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « דִּין הַשְׁאָלָה וְקִנְיַן צָרְכֵי שַׁבָּת וַהֲדָחַת הַכֵּלִים וְתִקּוּנָן וּטְבִילָתָן בְּשַׁבָּת וּבוֹ י״ב סְעִיפִים (chapeau) » → Laws of borrowing, of acquiring Shabbos needs, of washing and repairing vessels, and of their immersion on Shabbos. 12 seifim.
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « (פֵּרוּשׁ שֶׁרָגִיל הַמּוֹכֵר לִמְדּוֹד בּוֹ וְלִמְכּוֹר), וְאֵין בָּזֶה מִשּׁוּם קוֹנֶה קִנְיָן בְּשַׁבָּת בִּשְׁבִיל כָּךְ, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאַף הַמַּשְׁקֶה אֶת חֲבֵרוֹ בְּדֶרֶךְ מַתָּנָה ... בִּכְלִי הַמְיֻחָד לְמִדָּה » → — that is, the seller customarily measures with it and sells thereby. There is no prohibition here of acquiring a kinyan on Shabbos on that account, because even one who gives his fellow to drink by way of a gift sometimes also gives him to drink in a vessel designated for measuring.
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « (אֲבָל כְּשֶׁמַּזְכִּיר לוֹ שֵׁם מִדָּה ... וְאֵין בּוֹ מִשּׁוּם עוֹבָדִין דְּחֹל) » → (However, when he mentions to him the name of a measure — even if he is not buying from him at all but only borrowing, saying "lend me a measure of wine" — although there is no ground at all to forbid it on account of mekach u-memkar (buying and selling), there is nevertheless a prohibition of uvdin de-chol (weekday conduct) in that the lender measures out for him the measure he requests, as explained in siman 306: it is forbidden to measure anything on Shabbos because it is a weekday act and a disgrace to Shabbos. But when he does not mention the name of a measure, only "fill this vessel for me" without more — he does not appear to intend measuring, only to fill the vessel he requests, and there is no uvdin de-chol in it.)
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « בַּמֶּה דְּבָרִים אֲמוּרִים? כְּשֶׁנּוֹטֵל הַלּוֹקֵחַ ... (וְיֵשׁ בּוֹ מִשּׁוּם עוֹבָדִין דְּחֹל). » → When does this apply? When the buyer (or borrower) takes the measure of the seller (or of the lender) and carries it to his home; and all the more so if the buyer brings a measure from his home and says to him "fill this vessel for me", and the seller fills it from another vessel that is not of this measure. But to measure first in the vessel designated for measuring and then pour into the vessel of the buyer (or of the borrower) — is forbidden, because thereby it is evident that he intends to measure (and there is uvdin de-chol in it).
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְיֵשׁ מְקִלִּין לוֹמַר שֶׁכָּל שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְכַוֵּן וּמְצַמְצֵם הַמִּדָּה לְגַמְרֵי ... אוֹ שְׁאָר דָּבָר שֶׁאֵינוֹ מֻקְצֶה. » → Some are lenient, saying that as long as he does not calibrate the measure exactly — reducing or adding a little — it is permitted. There is no gezel (theft) in it, because the owners know that everyone does so on Shabbos and Yom Tov. And it is likewise the custom to drop a ring into the measure, or any other object that is not muktzeh.
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְיֵשׁ מַתִּירִין אֲפִלּוּ לְהַזְכִּיר שֵׁם דָּמִים וְשֵׁם מִדָּה ... שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לוֹ כַּמָּה שֶׁשָּׁוֶה כָּךְ וְכָךְ מָעוֹת. » → Others permit even mentioning the price and the name of the measure, provided only that he does not mention a total sum of money or a total of measures — that is, he shall not say to him "give me for such-and-such coins; you already have such-and-such by me, and you will have such-and-such by me", and likewise with a measure, "give me a measure, or such-and-such measures; you already have such-and-such by me, and you will have such-and-such by me" — for since he mentions the sum of money he owes him, it is evident that this is mekach u-memkar. Likewise, when he is exact about the total of measures, this is a weekday manner and resembles mekach u-memkar (and even one who borrows from his fellow is forbidden to speak so to him, because it resembles mekach u-memkar). But when he says "give me for such-and-such" — he does not appear to intend mekach u-memkar at all, only to let him know how much he needs; and the "such-and-such coins" he mentions is merely an indication: he needs as much as is worth such-and-such coins.
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְלֹא אָמְרוּ שֶׁאָסוּר לְהַזְכִּיר שֵׁם דָּמִים כְּלָל אֶלָּא בְּדָבָר שֶׁאֵין מִקָּחוֹ יָדוּעַ ... מֻתָּר לְהַזְכִּיר שֵׁם דָּמִים. » → And they said it is forbidden to mention the price at all only for an item whose price is not known, such as one who buys meat from the butcher: it is forbidden to say to him "give me a dinar's worth of meat", as will be explained in siman 500 — because one cannot say that he is merely informing him how much meat he needs, since he does not know how much they will give him for a dinar; and likewise in every similar case. But an item whose price is known — it is permitted to mention the price.
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְכֵן בְּמִדָּה, אֵין אִסּוּר לְהַזְכִּיר שֵׁם מִדָּה אֶלָּא בְּדָבָר שֶׁאֵין מִדָּתוֹ יָדוּעַ ... כֵּיוָן שֶׁאוֹמֵר לוֹ "מְדוֹד לִי"). » → And likewise with a measure: there is a prohibition on mentioning the name of a measure only for an item whose measure is not known, as when he says to him "give me a measure of nuts", and similar things that are not customarily measured — for then he does not know how much the measure holds, nor whether he will need more or not; so one cannot say that he is merely informing him how much he needs, and it is evident that he intends to take precisely by measure in order to know how much to pay him — which is a weekday manner and resembles mekach u-memkar. But an item whose measure is known, such as wine and beer and the like — it is permitted to mention to him the name of the measure (provided he says to him "give me such-and-such measures", but he shall not say to him "measure out for me such-and-such measures", for then it is evident that he intends to take precisely by measure, since he says to him "measure out for me").
- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « כַּסְבָרָא זוֹ ... וּבְהַזְכָּרַת שֵׁם מִדָּה בְּדָבָר שֶׁמִּדָּתוֹ יָדוּעַ. וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם, הַמּוֹכֵר צָרִיךְ לִזָּהֵר שֶׁלֹּא לְכַוֵּן הַמִּדָּה לְגַמְרֵי ... כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר לְמַעְלָה) » → according to this [latter] opinion ... and on mentioning the name of the measure for an item whose measure is known. In any case, the seller must take care not to calibrate the measure exactly, as explained above (and even if the buyer brought a measure from his home and the seller fills it for him — he shall not calibrate exactly, since the buyer mentions to him the name of a measure, as explained above). [Remplace l'ancienne fin « provided the seller does not calibrate the measure exactly, as a precaution against mekach u-memkar », dont la raison n'est pas dans la source.]
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וְכֵן לוֹמַר לְחֶנְוָנִי "תֵּן לִי עֶשְׂרִים בֵּיצִים אוֹ חֲמִשִּׁים אֱגוֹזִים". וְאֵין אוֹמְרִים שֶׁזֶּה דוֹמֶה לְמִקָּח וּמִמְכָּר ... שֶׁלֹּא יִטּוֹל אֶלָּא כְּפִי הַצֹּרֶךְ. » → and likewise to say to a shopkeeper: "Give me twenty eggs or fifty nuts". One does not say that this resembles mekach u-memkar, in that he mentions a number to him in order to know how much he will owe him — for even a householder in his own home customarily counts how much he takes, so as to take only according to need.
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, puis rendue littéralement sur וְיִהְיֶה לְךָ בְּיָדִי מֵאָה, où la FR écrit « pour que j'aie cent chez moi ») — « שֶׁלֹּא יֹאמַר לוֹ "הֲרֵי יֵשׁ לְךָ בְּיָדִי חֲמִשִּׁים אֱגוֹזִים, תֵּן לִי עוֹד חֲמִשִּׁים וְיִהְיֶה לְךָ בְּיָדִי מֵאָה", שֶׁהֲרֵי אֵין דַּרְכּוֹ שֶׁל בַּעַל הַבַּיִת לִהְיוֹת מְצַמְצֵם כָּךְ בְּתוֹךְ בֵּיתוֹ, וַהֲרֵי זֶה דוֹמֶה לְמִקָּח וּמִמְכָּר (וְאָסוּר אֲפִלּוּ אוֹמֵר בְּפֵרוּשׁ "הַשְׁאִילֵנִי") » → he shall not say to him "you already have fifty nuts by me; give me fifty more, and you will have a hundred by me", for it is not the way of a householder to be so exact in his own home, and this resembles mekach u-memkar (and it is forbidden even if he says explicitly "lend me"). [Remplace l'exemple entre parenthèses de l'ancienne version, « so I shall have a hundred by you », qui inversait le sens.]
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « לָתֵת לְתוֹכָן אַרְבָּעָה אוֹ חֲמִשָּׁה כַּדִּים ... בְּמָבוֹי שֶׁרַבִּים מְצוּיִים בּוֹ (וְגַם יַחְשְׁדוּהוּ שֶׁנּוֹשֵׂא מַשָּׂאוֹת לְצֹרֶךְ הַחֹל) ... וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → putting four or five jars into them, because this looks like a weekday act of carrying loads, and it is a disgrace to Shabbos when one does so in an alley frequented by the public (and people will also suspect him of carrying loads for weekday needs). ... in his hand or on his shoulder or the like
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם אֵין בְּשִׁנּוּי קַלּוּת בְּמַשָּׂא — צָרִיךְ לְשַׁנּוֹת, כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן תק״י ... וְאִם אִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹ לְשַׁנּוֹת, כְּגוֹן שֶׁזִּמֵּן אוֹרְחִים הַרְבֵּה וְצָרִיךְ לְמַהֵר לַהֲבִיאָם לִפְנֵיהֶם — מֻתָּר » → And even if the deviation brings no lightening of the load — he must deviate, as will be explained in siman 510 regarding Yom Tov; and the same applies to Shabbos in an alley with an eruv, for they are alike in this. And if it is impossible for him to deviate — for instance, he has invited many guests and must hurry to bring [the jars] before them — it is permitted.
- **séif 5, EN** (traduite de l hebreu) — « מִלְּהָבִיא כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד בִּפְנֵי עַצְמוֹ, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁמַּרְבֶּה בְּהִלּוּךְ » → rather than carrying each one by itself, because [that way] he multiplies walking. (Remplace « [rather than separately], because otherwise he multiplies needless walking ».)
- **séif 6, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אֲפִלּוּ מִיָּד אַחַר סְעֻדָּה שֶׁלְּפָנֶיהָ » → even immediately after the preceding meal
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אֲפִלּוּ הָיָה אֶפְשָׁר לְהַטְבִּילוֹ מֵעֶרֶב שַׁבָּת, וְאֵינוֹ חָשׁוּב כִּמְתַקֵּן כְּלִי, הוֹאִיל וּמִן הַתּוֹרָה מֻתָּר לְהִשְׁתַּמֵּשׁ בּוֹ בְּלֹא טְבִילָה, וְאֵין חִיּוּב טְבִילָתוֹ אֶלָּא מִדִּבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים » → even if it was possible to immerse it on Erev Shabbos, and this is not considered metakein keli, since by Torah law it is permitted to use it without immersion, and the obligation to immerse it is only mi-divrei soferim (rabbinic).
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אֲפִלּוּ לֹא הָיָה אֶפְשָׁר לְהַטְבִּילוֹ מֵעֶרֶב שַׁבָּת (אֲבָל בְּיוֹם טוֹב ... בְּסִימָן תק״ט). וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לוֹמַר לְדִבְרֵי הָאוֹמְרִים שֶׁטְּבִילַת כֵּלִים הִיא מִן הַתּוֹרָה ... כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״ב. » → even if it was not possible to immerse it on Erev Shabbos (whereas on Yom Tov it is permitted to immerse it when it was not possible for him to immerse on Erev Yom Tov, as will be explained in siman 509). And needless to say, according to those who say that tevilas keilim is a Torah requirement, as will be explained in Yoreh De'ah siman 120, this is an important tikkun according to all, and the Sages forbade it because it resembles actual metakein keli, which is an av melachah, as explained in siman 302.
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR (les parenthèses condensées de l'ancienne version remplacées par la phrase entière)) — « וְאָז אֵין צָרִיךְ טְבִילָה כְּלָל ... כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּיוֹרֶה דֵעָה שָׁם. / וְעָלְתָה לוֹ טְבִילָה. וְאֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כִּמְתַקֵּן ... לְשֵׁם טְבִילָה. / כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּיוֹרֶה דֵעָה שָׁם » → and then no immersion is required at all, for it is permitted to give a gift on Shabbos when it is for the needs of Shabbos, as explained in siman 306. Nevertheless, after Shabbos he must immerse it without a berachah, as explained in Yoreh De'ah there. / and the immersion takes effect for him. This does not appear as metakein, because it is not evident that he intends to immerse it: one who sees it says "he needed to draw this water", for not everyone knows that he has not yet immersed it. And he shall not recite a berachah on this immersion, so that it not be evident that he intends it for the sake of tevilah. / as will be explained in Yoreh De'ah there.
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם, אִם עָבַר וְהִטְבִּיל כֵּלִים בְּשַׁבָּת — אֵין קוֹנְסִין אוֹתוֹ ... כֵּיוָן שֶׁיֵּשׁ מַתִּירִין אֲפִלּוּ לְכַתְּחִלָּה » → Nevertheless, if one transgressed and immersed vessels on Shabbos — he is not penalized by being forbidden to use them that same day, as is the law for one who performs a melachah on Shabbos, even a rabbinic one, that he may not benefit from it until evening, as explained in siman 318 — since there are those who permit it even lechatchilah.
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « מִכָּל מָקוֹם ... וְאֵין אוֹסְרִים טִלְטוּלוֹ כְּלָל » → nevertheless ... and do not forbid moving it at all.
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « שֶׁהַכֶּסֶף הוּא רַךְ נֶגְדּוֹ » → since the silver is soft relative to it
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁגַּם הֵם גּוֹרְרִים אֶת הַכֶּסֶף וּמְמַחֲקִין אוֹתוֹ לִפְעָמִים, מִכָּל מָקוֹם » → although they too scrape the silver and efface it at times, nevertheless,

### Siman 324

- **séif 1, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « דִּינֵי הֲכָנַת מַאֲכָל לִבְהֵמָה בְּשַׁבָּת וּבוֹ י״ב סְעִיפִים: … דֶּרֶךְ נִקְבֵי הַכְּבָרָה, כֵּיוָן שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְכַוֵּן לְכָךְ וְלֹא "פְּסִיק רֵישֵׁהּ" הוּא » → Laws of preparing food for an animal on Shabbos, containing 12 seifim: … — even though the chaff sometimes falls of its own accord through the holes of the sieve, since one does not intend this and it is not a pesik reishei (inevitable consequence) — is permitted.
- **séif 2, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « אַף שֶׁלֹּא בִּמְקוֹם מִקָּח וּמִמְכָּר, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״ו » → even where it is not a matter of buying and selling, as was explained in siman 306.
- **séif 3, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « כְּבָר נִתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן שכ״א … וְאִם גְּבָלוֹ בְּשַׁבָּת — יֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים שֶׁהוּא פָּטוּר מִן הַתּוֹרָה, אֲבָל … אוֹ לַתַּרְנְגוֹלִים … כְּגוֹן לְגָבְלוֹ מְעַט מְעַט … אוֹ אֲפִלּוּ הַרְבֵּה בְּבַת אַחַת אֶלָּא שֶׁלֹּא יְמָרֵס בְּיָדוֹ … אֲפִלּוּ כַּמָּה פְעָמִים עַד שֶׁיִּתְעָרֵב יָפֶה … הֲרֵי זֶה שִׁנּוּי גָּמוּר. וּמֻתָּר גַּם כֵּן לְנַעֲרוֹ מִכְּלִי אֶל כְּלִי … אֲפִלּוּ כּוֹר וַאֲפִלּוּ כּוֹרַיִם … וּלְדִבְרֵי הָאוֹמְרִים שֶׁהַמְגַבֵּל הַמֻּרְסָן חַיָּב מִן הַתּוֹרָה … אָסוּר לִתֵּן מַיִם לְמֻרְסָן בְּשַׁבָּת אֲפִלּוּ עַל יְדֵי שִׁנּוּי, אֶלָּא אִם נָתַן בּוֹ הַמַּיִם מֵעֶרֶב שַׁבָּת — אֲזַי אֵין בְּגִבּוּלוֹ בְּשַׁבָּת אִסּוּר מִן הַתּוֹרָה … וּכְבָר נִתְבָּאֵר שָׁם שֶׁיֵּשׁ לְהַחֲמִיר כַּסְבָרָא זוֹ, וְכֵן נוֹהֲגִין » → It was already explained in siman 321 that bran (mursan) is among the things that are not subject to kneading (bnei gibbul), and if one kneaded it on Shabbos, some say that one is exempt by Torah law; but it is rabbinically forbidden to knead bran for one's animal or for chickens in the weekday manner. Only with a shinui (change) — for example, kneading it little by little, as was explained there; or even a large amount at once, provided one does not mash it by hand after putting the water into it in the weekday manner, but rather passes a ladle or stick through it crosswise (shesi va-erev), even several times, until it is well mixed. For since one does not mash it by hand, and does not turn the ladle or the stick around in a circle in the weekday manner but passes it crosswise, this is a complete change. It is likewise permitted to shake it from one vessel to another until it is well mixed. And it is permitted to mix bran in this manner in a single vessel, even a kor or even two korim, according to what one needs for one's animals that day, and to divide it into many vessels and place it before each and every animal. And according to those who say that one who kneads bran is liable by Torah law, and that even by merely adding water without kneading one is liable — for adding the water is its kneading, as was explained there — it is forbidden to add water to bran on Shabbos even with a change. Unless the water was added on erev Shabbos: then kneading it on Shabbos carries no Torah prohibition, only a rabbinic one, and they permitted it with a change in the manner explained. And it was already explained there that one should be stringent in accordance with this view, and such is the custom.
- **séif 4, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « בִּשְׁנַיִם וּשְׁלֹשָׁה מְקוֹמוֹת … כְּדֶרֶךְ שֶׁעוֹשִׂים בְּאָכְלֵי בְהֵמָה כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהוּ נוֹחִים לְאָכְלָם, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁכָּל דָּבָר שֶׁהוּא אֹכֶל, דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁהוּא רָאוּי לַאֲכִילָה בְּלֹא תִקּוּן — אָסוּר לִטְרוֹחַ בּוֹ לְהַכְשִׁירוֹ וּלְתַקְּנוֹ יוֹתֵר לְתַעֲנוּג בְּעָלְמָא … דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁאֵינוֹ רָאוּי לַאֲכִילָה בְּלֹא אֵיזֶה תִּקּוּן שֶׁאֵין בּוֹ סְרַךְ מְלָאכָה — מֻתָּר לְתַקְּנוֹ וְלַעֲשׂוֹתוֹ אֹכֶל, כְּגוֹן עֵצִים שֶׁקְּצָצָן מִן הָאִילָן מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם … וְהוּא שֶׁנִּתְלְשׁוּ מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם, אֲבָל אִם נִתְלְשׁוּ בְּשַׁבָּת, אֲפִלּוּ עַל יְדֵי נָכְרִי שֶׁתְּלָשָׁן לְעַצְמוֹ — אֲסוּרִים בְּטִלְטוּל, כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן שכ״ה » → Bundles of stalks of grain that have been tied in two or three places — … But it is forbidden to rub them by hand, as is done with animal fodder so that it be easy to eat, for anything that is food — that is, fit to be eaten without preparation — it is forbidden to exert oneself over it to make it more fit and improve it further, for mere pleasure. However, something that is not food — that is, not fit to be eaten without some preparation that has no trace of a melachah — it is permitted to prepare it and make it into food. For example, branches that were cut from the tree while it was still day, which some feed to animals while they are still moist, rubbing them by hand to feed them, since they are not fit without rubbing — it is permitted to rub them on Shabbos. And likewise squash that is hard — it is permitted to cut it before the animal, which cannot eat it without cutting. This is provided they were detached while it was still day; but if they were detached on Shabbos, even by a non-Jew who detached them for himself, they are forbidden in tiltul (moving), as will be explained in siman 325.
- **séif 5, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « וְאֵין בָּהּ מִשּׁוּם מֻקְצֶה, אֲפִלּוּ לֹא הָיְתָה חוֹלָה כְּלָל מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם … כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְבָּאֵר בְּסִימָן ש״י. בַּמֶּה דְּבָרִים אֲמוּרִים? בִּנְבֵלָה הַקָּשָׁה, שֶׁאִי אֶפְשָׁר לָהֶם לְאָכְלָהּ בְּלֹא חֲתִיכָה, וַאֲפִלּוּ נֶאֱכֶלֶת לָהֶם עַל יְדֵי הַדְּחָק … (וְעַיֵּן סִימָן שכ״א …). וְכֵן אֵין חוֹתְכִים שַׁחַת וְחָרוּבִים לִפְנֵי בְהֵמָה בֵּין גַּסָּה בֵּין דַּקָּה … לָהֶם … וְכֵן כָּל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָזֶה » → … and there is no issue of muktzeh in it, even if it was not sick at all while it was still day, so that one's mind was not at all on it for the dogs while it was still day — because there is no muktzeh in a thing from which a person has diverted his mind, unless he actively pushed it away with his hands, as was explained in siman 310. When does this apply? In the case of a hard carcass, which they cannot eat without cutting; and even if they can eat it with difficulty without cutting, it is permitted to exert oneself to cut it further for them, as was explained above (and see siman 321 on whether it is permitted to cut raw meat very finely before the fowl). Likewise, one does not cut fresh fodder (shachas) or carobs before an animal, whether large or small, since they are suitable for them without cutting. And so too in every similar case.
- **séif 6, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁמַּאֲכִילוֹ בְּיָדוֹ הַרְבֵּה כָּל כָּךְ עַד שֶׁמַּרְחִיבִים בְּנֵי מֵעָיו כְּאֵבוּס, שֶׁזֶּהוּ טוֹרֵחַ שֶׁלֹּא לְצֹרֶךְ הַיּוֹם … דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁדּוֹרֵס לוֹ מַאֲכָל בִּגְרוֹנוֹ לְמָקוֹם שֶׁאֵין הַגָּמָל יָכוֹל לְהַחֲזִירוֹ, שֶׁזֶּהוּ גַם כֵּן טוֹרֵחַ יוֹתֵר … כְּשֶׁיִּרְצֶה. וְכֵן הָעֲגָלִים — אֵין מְאַמְּרִין אוֹתָם, דְּהַיְנוּ לִתְחוֹב לְמָקוֹם שֶׁאֵינָן יְכוֹלִים לְהַחֲזִירוֹ, אֲבָל מַלְעִיטִין לְמָקוֹם שֶׁיְּכוֹלִים לְהַחֲזִירוֹ, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁכַּוָּנָתוֹ כְּדֵי לְפַטְּמָן. וְכֵן הַדִּין בְּאַוָּזִין וְתַרְנְגוֹלִים, שֶׁמֻּתָּר לִתְחוֹב הַמַּאֲכָל בְּפִיהֶם לְמָקוֹם שֶׁיְּכוֹלִים לְהַחֲזִירוֹ. וְכֵן בְּיוֹנִים בַּיְתִיּוֹת » → One does not force-feed (ovsin) the camel — that is, feeding it by hand so much that its innards are widened like a trough — for that is exertion not for the needs of the day. Nor does one stuff it (dorsim) — that is, pressing food into its throat to a spot from which the camel cannot disgorge it — for that too is greater exertion. But one may mal'it it — that is, place the food in a spot from which it can disgorge it when it wishes. Likewise calves: one does not me'amer them — that is, place food in a spot from which they cannot disgorge it — but one may mal'it them in a spot from which they can disgorge it, even though one's intention is to fatten them. And the same law applies to geese and chickens: it is permitted to insert the food into their mouths in a spot from which they can disgorge it. And likewise with domestic pigeons.
- **séif 7, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu ; sujet de וּמַשְׁהֶה rendu selon l'hébreu, voir doutes)) — « וְכֵן שְׁאָר עוֹפוֹת אוֹ … מַיִם וְלֹא מְזוֹנוֹת … וְיוֹנֵי עֲלִיָּה, וַאֲפִלּוּ לְהַשְׁלִיךְ לִפְנֵיהֶם אָסוּר, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵין מְזוֹנָתָן עָלֶיךָ, שֶׁיּוֹצְאִים וְאוֹכְלִים בַּשָּׂדֶה … שֶׁמְּזוֹנוֹתָיו עָלֶיךָ … שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְגַדְּלוֹ בְּבֵיתוֹ — מִכָּל מָקוֹם … שֶׁהֲרֵי הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא חָס עָלָיו עַל שֶׁמְּזוֹנוֹתָיו מֻעָטִים וּמַשְׁהֶה אֲכִילָתוֹ בְּמֵעָיו שְׁלֹשָׁה יָמִים. אֲבָל אֵין נוֹתְנִין מְזוֹנוֹת לִפְנֵי חֲזִיר, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵין מְזוֹנוֹתָיו עָלֶיךָ, שֶׁהֲרֵי אָמְרוּ "אָרוּר הָאִישׁ מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁמְּגַדֵּל חֲזִירִים" » → Likewise other fowl or animals raised in the house — … But one does not give water or food to bees, nor to dovecote-pigeons or attic-pigeons, and it is forbidden even to throw [food] before them, since their sustenance is not upon you, for they go out and eat in the field. But one does give food before a dog, since its sustenance is upon you. And even an ownerless (hefker) dog that one does not raise in one's house — nevertheless there is a slight mitzvah to give it a little food, for the Holy One, blessed be He, has compassion on it because its food is scant, and keeps its food in its innards for three days. But one does not give food before a pig, since its sustenance is not upon you, for they said: "Cursed be the man of Israel who raises pigs."
- **séif 9, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « שֶׁאֵין לוֹ מַה שֶּׁיֹּאכַל כִּי אִם מַה שֶּׁיַּאֲכִילוֹ הָאָדָם » → for it has nothing to eat except what a person feeds it.
- **séif 10, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאָדָם מְצֻוֶּה עַל שְׁבִיתַת בְּהֶמְתּוֹ, מֻתָּר לְהַנִּיחָהּ שֶׁתֹּאכַל מֵהַמְחֻבָּר בְּשַׁבָּת, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "לְמַעַן יָנוּחַ שׁוֹרְךָ וגו׳", וְזוֹ הִיא נְיָחָה שֶׁלָּהּ. וּמֻתָּר אֲפִלּוּ לְהַעֲמִידָהּ בְּיָדָיו … וְאֵין חוֹשְׁשִׁים שֶׁמָּא יִתְלוֹשׁ מֵהֶם בְּיָדָיו לְהַאֲכִילָהּ, לְפִי שֶׁהוּא אִסּוּר חָמוּר בְּעֵינֵי הַבְּרִיּוֹת … מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאִסּוּרוֹ קַל בְּעֵינֵי הַבְּרִיּוֹת, וְחוֹשְׁשִׁים שֶׁמָּא יִטּוֹל מִמֶּנּוּ בְּיָדָיו לְהַאֲכִילָהּ. וְלֹא אָסְרוּ אֶלָּא לְהַעֲמִידָהּ עַל גַּבָּהּ מַמָּשׁ, אֲבָל מֻתָּר לַעֲמוֹד בְּפָנֶיהָ … אֵין לָחוּשׁ » → Although a person is commanded concerning the resting (shevisah) of his animal, it is permitted to let it eat from what is attached [to the ground] on Shabbos, as it says: "that your ox may rest, etc." — and this is its rest. And it is permitted even to stand it with one's hands upon attached grasses, and we do not fear lest one detach some of them with one's hands to feed it, since that is a prohibition grave in people's eyes. But one does not stand it upon something forbidden in tiltul (moving) by rabbinic law, since its prohibition is light in people's eyes, and we fear lest one take some of it with one's hands to feed it. And they forbade only standing it directly upon it; but it is permitted to stand in front of it in such a way that it can turn only in that direction, and it eats on its own — for since one is not standing near the grasses, there is no concern.
- **séif 11, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « מִפְּנֵי שֶׁרָאוּי הוּא לוֹ, שֶׁהַחֲמוֹר אֵינוֹ מֵטִיל רִירִין וְאֵין מַה שֶּׁלְּפָנָיו נִמְאָס, וְרָאוּי הוּא (לְחַתְּכוֹ) לְתִתּ[וֹ] לִפְנֵי הַשּׁוֹר … וְאֵינוֹ רָאוּי עוֹד לַחֲמוֹר אֶלָּא עַל יְדֵי הַדְּחָק, וְקָרוֹב לְוַדַּאי שֶׁלֹּא יֹאכְלֶנּוּ, וְנִמְצָא טוֹרֵחַ בְּחִנָּם בְּשַׁבָּת. וְיֵשׁ מַחֲמִירִים גַּם כֵּן בִּשְׁאָר מִינֵי בְהֵמָה … וְאֵין לִמְחוֹת בְּיַד הַנּוֹהֲגִין הֶתֵּר » → … since it is fit for it: the donkey does not drool saliva, and what is before it is not made repulsive, and it is fit (to cut it) to place it before the ox. But one does not take from before an ox to place before a donkey, since it has become soiled by the saliva of the ox and is no longer fit for the donkey except under duress; it is almost certain that it will not eat it, and one would thus be exerting oneself for nothing on Shabbos. And some are stringent also with other kinds of animals, that one should not take from before one to give before another that is not of its kind. But one should not protest against those who conduct themselves leniently.
- **séif 12, EN** (portée depuis la page FR (confrontée à l'hébreu)) — « וְגוֹרְפִים הָאֵבוּס שֶׁלְּפָנָיו לָתֵת לְתוֹכוֹ הַתֶּבֶן וְהַשְּׂעוֹרִים שֶׁלֹּא יִתְעָרֵב בָּהֶם עַפְרוּרִית וְיָקוּץ בְּמַאֲכָלוֹ … אֲפִלּוּ הוּא אֵבוּס שֶׁל כְּלִי … וְשֶׁבַּקַּרְקַע יֵשׁ לָחוּשׁ שֶׁמָּא כְּשֶׁיִּגְרְפוֹ וְיִרְאֶה בוֹ גּוּמוֹת — יַשְׁוֶה אוֹתָן הַגּוּמוֹת בְּמִתְכַּוֵּן כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִפְּלוּ הַשְּׂעוֹרִים לְתוֹכָן. וְאָסוּר גַּם כֵּן לְסַלֵּק הַתֶּבֶן מִלְּפָנָיו כְּשֶׁהוּא רַב, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִדְרְסֶנּוּ בִּרְעִי, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ תֶּבֶן שֶׁנִּמְאָס בְּמִדְרַס רַגְלָיו וְאֵינוֹ רָאוּי לוֹ עוֹד, וְהוּא אָסוּר בְּטִלְטוּל » → An ox being fattened, whose trough before it is scraped (gorfin) to put the straw and barley into it, so that no dust mixes with them and it becomes disgusted with its food — it is forbidden to scrape it on Shabbos, even if it is a trough that is a vessel, by decree on account of a trough at ground level. And with one at ground level there is concern lest, when one scrapes it and sees depressions in it, one deliberately even out those depressions so that the barley does not fall into them. It is likewise forbidden to remove the straw from before it when it is plentiful, so that it does not trample it with excrement, because there is straw in it that has become repulsive through the trampling of its feet and is no longer fit for it, and it is forbidden in tiltul (moving).

### Siman 328

- **séif 1, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אלא על דרך שיתבאר | ואין בו שום אסור כלל לבריא | שמתכון לרפואה | לרפואה כשיהיה מתר לעסוק ברפואות בשבת » → …except in the manner that will be explained. / …and that carries no prohibition at all for a healthy person … and the thing is recognizable as intended for healing … lest he come to grind ingredients for healing, when it would be permitted to engage in remedies on Shabbos
- **séif 2, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « והנשאל הרי זה מגנה שהיה לו לדרוש ברבים שמתר | אשר יעשה האדם | מה תלמוד לומר וחי בהם כלומר ראה | ולא שיבא על ידי כך לידי ספק סכנה | ומכל מקום אפלו בודאי סכנה… וכשהיא רפואה ידועה… מחללין מספק » → and the one who is asked — behold this is contemptible, for he should have expounded publicly that it is permitted. / "asher ya'aseh ha-adam vachai bahem" — what does "vachai bahem" teach? That is to say: see that he should live definitely through the performance of mitzvos, and not that he come through them to a doubt of danger. / Nonetheless, even in a case of definite danger — one desecrates only with a remedy that is known to all, or on the word of an expert (mumcheh). And when it is a known remedy, even though it is not known whether this person will be healed by it or not — one desecrates out of doubt
- **séif 3, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « והוא שנתקלקלו השנים… או שיש בהם בועא… | ואין מחללין עליהם אפלו אם כואב לו מאד | אזי | ואם אינו מצטער כל כך מתר לעשות על ידי נכרי כל דבר האסור משום שבות… (99 mots, jusqu'à la fin du séif) » → This is provided that the teeth, or one of the other internal organs, have been damaged as a result of an injury, or that there is an abscess (bu'a) in them and the like … and one does not desecrate for them even if he is in great pain. / (and if he is not in such great pain — it is permitted to do through a non-Jew anything that is forbidden as a shevus), as will be explained. Therefore one whose tooth aches and who is in such pain that his whole body becomes sick from it (then it is permitted to desecrate through a non-Jew) — tells a non-Jew to extract it. / And even though the Jew presents his tooth to him and helps somewhat in extracting it — this is of no consequence, for one who assists has no substance (mesaye'a ein bo mamash), since even had the Jew not helped him with this help, but merely not hindered him — the non-Jew could have done this thing by himself. / (And according to those who say that a melachah she-einah tzerichah legufah is forbidden only by the words of the Sages — it is permitted to extract it through a non-Jew even if his whole body has not become sick from it, as will be explained)
- **séif 4, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu ; texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 4) — « אלא אפלו בסתם שאין שם בקיאין שמכירין בה שצריך חלול… | ממאכלים ורפואות שהם יפים לחולה אף על פי שאין סכנה כלל… | ואין צריך חלול | וכל שכן אם החולה או הבקי אומר שאין צריך ויש אומרים… (132 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → even in an ordinary case where there are no experts present who recognize that it requires desecration, and the choleh too says nothing … in foods and remedies that are good for the choleh, even though there is no danger at all in withholding that thing, since in any case the illness involves danger … and does not require desecration … and all the more so if the choleh or the expert says that it is not needed. / And some say that even where desecration is needed, one desecrates through a Jew only for something regarding which there is concern that if it is not done for him he will be endangered; but anything whose withholding involves no danger … is done on Shabbos only through non-Jews, like the law of the needs of a choleh who is not in danger. / (And so one should conduct oneself in these lands … if there is no non-Jew there at all — one may rely on the first opinion. And nonetheless, every ba'al nefesh should be concerned for himself regarding a Torah prohibition)
- **séif 5, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « או שאומר שאם לא יחללו עליו אפשר שיכביד עליו חליו והוא בענין שיש לחוש שאם יכביד עליו חליו אפשר שמסתכן … ואם לאו אין מחללין » → or says that if Shabbos is not desecrated his illness may grow worse, and it is in a situation where there is concern that if his illness grows worse he may be endangered — one desecrates; and if not — one does not desecrate
- **séif 6, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אפלו עלתה שם מאליה שלא מחמת הכאת ברזל | והיא תולעת קטנה ומצויה במים… | אם לאו » → even if it arose there by itself and not from a blow of iron … which is a small worm commonly found in water, and when it reaches a person's flesh it sucks the blood until it becomes like a small barrel, and when a person swallows it in water it sucks the moisture of his innards until his belly swells … or not
- **séif 7, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שלא על גב היד והרגל | והוא הנקרא פלונקר״ו בלע״ז | אפלו אינו חם ביותר אלא שהוא עם סימור דהינו (au lieu de « קדחת ») | שקורין שוידרי״ן בלשון אשכנז… אבל קדחת המצוי שתחלתו קר וסופו חם אין בו סכנה » → even though it is on the outer flesh and not on the back of the hand or the foot … (which is called plonkro in the vernacular) … or even one that is not very hot but comes with shivering (simur), namely a fever with chills (which is called shvideren in the language of Ashkenaz) — that is, when the heat and the cold come upon him at one time. But the ordinary fever, whose beginning is cold and whose end is hot — involves no danger
- **séif 8, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « (בענין שיש בו חשש סכנה) | ואם אין בו חשש סכנה מתר לו להצטנן במים מפני שנראה כמקר ולא כמתכון לרפואה » → (in a manner that involves a suspicion of danger) … (And if there is no suspicion of danger — he is permitted to cool himself in water, because he appears as one cooling himself and not as one intending a refuah)
- **séif 9, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שמגליד | או שהכאב נועץ בה כמו מחט… | של כל אלו | שאז יש סכנה שאם תפקע העין ימות | אבל בסוף החלי של כל אלו… כמו שיתבאר » → in which there is mucus that keeps forming a crust, or in which the pain pierces like a needle, or which burns and is hot — one desecrates at the beginning of the illness in all of these, for then there is danger: if the eye should burst, he would die … / But at the end of the illness in all of these — namely when he is already close to recovering and only a little of the illness remains, burning slightly — one does not do, even through a non-Jew, anything but what is only a shevus, such as applying an eye-salve of ingredients ground the day before, as will be explained
- **séif 10, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אף על פי שהוא על הבשר מבחוץ | חלול (x2) | אלא שאחד אומר שהוא מכיר בחלי זה… | וספק נפשות להקל ואפלו אם אומר שיש להסתפק… | לחלל שבת על פיהם כשאין שם ישראל המכיר חלי זה » → even though it is on the outer flesh … that desecration is needed … that it is not needed … / And even if there is no expert physician there, but one person says that he recognizes this illness and that it seems to him that it requires desecration — one desecrates on his word, for all people are considered somewhat expert, and any doubt of danger to life is treated leniently. (And even if he says that there is room for doubt whether it perhaps requires desecration — one desecrates. This is provided that he says that he recognizes this illness.) Nonetheless, he is not believed to contradict the expert who says that it does not require desecration, even if he says that it certainly requires desecration. / And all this is regarding a Jew. But ordinary non-Jews who are not physicians — they are not considered expert enough to desecrate Shabbos on their word, when there is no Jew there who recognizes this illness
- **séif 11, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « מפני שאינו רוצה שיחללו עליו שבת (passage rendu ; la porte ne l'avait pas listé à part) » → because he does not want Shabbos to be desecrated for him
- **séif 12, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « שהלב יודע מרת נפשו » → for "the heart knows the bitterness of its soul" (lev yode'a maras nafsho)
- **séif 13, EN** (traduite de l'hebreu, guidee par la page FR ; abregee ici, texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 13) — « ונמצא שלא נחלל עליו אלא שבת אחת | שאף שבודאי לא ימות היום… (240 mots, à la place d'une phrase condensée) | ויבאו להקל שלא במקום פקוח נפש… (172 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → "…and it will turn out that we desecrate only one Shabbos for him" … For even though he will certainly not die today … there is concern that he may die after the eight days if they do not begin the remedy for him immediately. / But where it is possible to do the remedy for him immediately without Shabbos desecration, except that they would need to wait a short while — they should not desecrate … for Shabbos is set aside (dechuyah) in the face of pikuach nefesh and not entirely permitted (hutrah) … / And nonetheless, even if there are before us non-Jews or minors … there is no desecration here at all. / Moreover, even if the Jew comes to be stringent with himself … there is a prohibition in the matter by the words of the Sages … / And even with women … they join together with a Jew … / And nonetheless, it is the choicest mitzvah … so that Shabbos should not be light in their eyes and they come to be lenient where there is no pikuach nefesh, and also in order to teach the practical halachah in public. / And some say that since Shabbos is set aside and not permitted at all … for then there is no Torah prohibition. And if it is possible to do it through a non-Jew without any lateness at all — it must be done through a non-Jew by Torah law … / And the main [ruling] is like the first opinion. And even so, the custom in these lands is like the latter opinion. But it is good not to conduct oneself so … one who wishes to do it through a non-Jew — should make known publicly on that occasion that there is a permission for the Jew himself, except that the non-Jew happens to be at hand here
- **séif 14, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « וכן כל כיוצא בזה (et l'exemple du filet : כגון שפרש מצודה להעלות תינוק… ועלו עמו דגים) » → even if along with it he accomplishes something else; for example, one who spread a net to bring up a child who fell into the river, and caught fish along with him, and anything similar
- **séif 16, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שהיא אסור לאו ואל נחלל עליו שבת שהיא אסור סקילה… (58 mots) | והשחוטה מתאחרת לו | אבל אם הוא צריך להרתיח לו יין ימלא ישראל ויחם נכרי… (70 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → and one does not say "let us feed him neveilah, which is a prohibition of a lav, and let us not desecrate Shabbos for him, which is a prohibition punishable by stoning (sekilah)"; for Shabbos has already been given to be set aside for kindling and for cooking. Moreover, with neveilah he transgresses on every single kezayis … Moreover, perhaps the choleh will be repelled by eating something forbidden, and will hold back and be endangered. / …while the slaughtered meat would be delayed for him … / But if he needs wine to be heated for him — a Jew should fill it and a non-Jew should heat it, according to the custom explained above. And he should watch over him (see Yoreh De'ah, siman 153 and 155) that he not touch it before it boils … And there is no concern at all that the choleh will be repelled by it, since its prohibition is not so severe
- **séif 17, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « ויש להם שכר מאת ה׳ אף על פי שנתרפא בראשונה » → and they have reward from Hashem, even if he recovered with the first
- **séif 18, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שאף שמרבה בגרוגרות מכל מקום ממעט בתלישה… אבל אם היו שתים בעקץ אחד ושלש בעקץ אחד לא יכרתו אלא העקץ שיש בו שתים… | שלא יבא לידי דחוי ועכוב » → for even though he takes more figs, nonetheless he lessens the detaching (telishah), which is the essence of the melachah. / But if there were two on one stem and three on one stem — they should cut only the stem that has two, for it is forbidden to increase the quantity of the thing with which the melachah is done, even though he does not increase the effort of the melachah, since it is all done at one time. / …so that it not come to delay and hindrance
- **séif 19, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu ; abregee ici, texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 19) — « שאז אף על פי שהולך כנפל למשכב הוא דומה | כגון לאפות לו או לבשל לו… (50 mots) | כל שאין בו סכנת נפש | בידים כגון לעשות לו כל רפואה… (35 mots) | לעשותה לו | מדרך החל | et 5 autres écarts (fin du séif : שנוי, הגונח, מאכלים הנכרים, ויש אוסרין, בדברי סופרים הלך אחר המקל) » → for then, even though he walks about, he is like one who fell to bed … such as baking for him or cooking for him if he needs it. And even though all forbidden foods … nonetheless they permitted him the prohibition of food cooked by non-Jews (bishulei nochrim) on Shabbos … / …as long as there is no danger to life (sakanas nefesh). / …with his own hands — such as doing for him any refuah that is forbidden because of the decree of grinding ingredients … it is permitted to do it for him without any alteration from the weekday manner, if there is danger to a limb, even though he did not fall to bed and his whole body has not become sick from it. / But if there is no danger to a limb … except with an alteration from the weekday manner (shinui). And through a shinui it is permitted to do even a full melachah, such as one who groans [with heart pain, who is permitted to suckle] with his mouth … / And if he needs to eat foods that are recognizable as being for refuah … they permitted it to him. And some forbid. And as to the halachah, in matters of the words of the Sages follow the lenient view
- **séif 20, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « (אבל על כל פנים יש לו צער גדול) … אפלו הוא מלאכה אבל אסור לו לאכול מאכלים הנכרים… (113 mots) | כמו שנתבאר למעלה » → (but in any case he has great pain) … even if it is a melachah. But he is forbidden to eat foods recognizable as being for refuah, and all the more so other shevusim without a shinui … and likewise to do a full melachah through a non-Jew, for that is a full shevus, without a shinui. / And if he has no great pain nor an illness that encompasses the whole body, but only a partial illness — then it is permitted for him to do any shevus of the words of the Sages through a non-Jew, even without a shinui, as was explained in siman 307, but not through a Jew, even with a shinui. Therefore it is permitted to do for him an external refuah through a non-Jew who places it on him there … (but it is forbidden to eat foods recognizable as being for refuah, in the manner that will be explained). / …as was explained above
- **séif 21, EN** (traduite de l'hebreu, guidee par la page FR ; abregee ici, texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 21) — « כגון נכרי הכוחל לעין ישראל בסוף החלי… | כל שהיה יכול הנכרי לעשותו בלבדו… (200 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → for example, a non-Jew who applies eye-salve to the eye of a Jew at the end of the illness, when it burns only slightly — he is permitted to open and close the eye so that the salve enters it well, for one who assists has no substance … / And even when the non-Jew does for him a full melachah where it is permitted — the choleh is permitted to assist him in this manner that was explained. / (And even though assisting in a full melachah is a full shevus of the words of the Sages, as will be explained in siman 346 … nonetheless the Sages did not require for this that it be imposed on the non-Jew and that the one to whom it pertains to do it, namely the choleh himself, should not do it. / But when the non-Jew does some other forbidden thing … it is permitted even for another Jew to assist him slightly). / And if it cannot be done at all without the Jew's help — it is forbidden even for the choleh himself to assist him
- **séif 22, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « ומתר להאכילו מקצה אפלו בידים אם אי אפשר בענין אחר » → And it is permitted to feed him muktzeh, even with one's hands, if it is impossible otherwise
- **séif 24, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אף על פי שיש בו סכנה מכל מקום אין בצנה סכנה לו שאפשר לחממו בבגדים לכן | אלא אם כן אין שם בגדים… | ולהאומרים שכל חולה שיש בו סכנה… (46 mots) » → even though he is in danger, nonetheless the cold involves no danger for him, since it is possible to warm him with garments; therefore … unless there are no garments there to warm him with. / And according to those who say that for any choleh in danger one does through a Jew everything that is usually done for him on a weekday, even though there is no danger in withholding that thing — (nonetheless) it is permitted to make him a medurah too through a Jew, in its weekday manner (and it was already explained above how one should conduct oneself in these lands)
- **séif 25, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « ולא כמתכון לרפואה | והוא שאינו פותח וסוגר העין אבל אם פותחה וסוגרה… ועכשו שאין דרך לרחוץ ביין אסור בכל ענין כשמתכון לרפואה » → and not as intending refuah. This is provided that he does not open and close the eye; but if he opens and closes it so that the wine enters it — the matter is recognizable as intending refuah, and it is forbidden. And now that it is not customary to wash with wine — it is forbidden in every manner when he intends refuah
- **séif 26, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « דהינו כל שלא טעם כלום משנעור משנתו | שאינו נראה כרוחץ שאין דרך לרחוץ ברק מפני מאיסותו אבל אם (au lieu de « ואם ») | אף על פי שרק תפל מערב בהם | מפני שאינן מאוסין… ואם אינו יכול לפתוח עיניו מתר ללחלחם אפלו ברק תפל לבדו… » → namely whenever he has not tasted anything since he awoke from his sleep … for he does not appear as washing, since it is not customary to wash with saliva because of its repulsiveness. But if … even though fasting saliva is mixed in it … because it is not repulsive to wash with. And if he cannot open his eyes — it is permitted to moisten them even with fasting saliva alone, for they forbade only when he intends refuah, but this is not refuah
- **séif 27, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שאסור לעסוק ברפואות בשבת ואין לחוש למראית העין… (54 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → he has a reminder (heker) in this that it is forbidden to engage in remedies on Shabbos. And there is no concern for appearances (maris ha-ayin), because it appears only as washing, for since it is soft and clear — the onlooker thinks it is wine. Provided only that he does not open and close the eye, for then it is evident that he intends refuah. But a thick collyrium — it is forbidden to apply to the eye on Shabbos, because it is recognizable that it is for refuah. And if he placed it on [the eye] while it was still day — it is permitted, as was explained in siman 252
- **séif 28, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « במקומות שדרכן לסוך אף שלא לרפואה כמו שנתבאר בסימן שכ״ז | ואסור מטעם שנתבאר בסימן שכ״ו | דהינו שכבר נתרפאה ואין לו צער ממנה | אבל אין נותנין שמן וחמין מערבים יחד… (95 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → in places where it is customary to anoint even not for refuah, as was explained in siman 327 … and it is forbidden for the reason explained in siman 326 … namely when it has already healed and he has no pain from it … / But one does not place oil and hot water mixed together upon a wound, nor upon a wad (moch) to place it on it, because it is recognizable that he intends refuah. But he places them on his flesh outside the wound … and they flow down onto the wound. But it is permitted to put oil alone on it … And likewise it is permitted to put hot water alone on it, if it was heated on Friday; and likewise (it is permitted to put oil alone) on a wad to place it on it. (But not hot water, even alone (if the wad (see siman 301) is not designated for this), because of the decree lest he wring it out. But with oil one does not decree lest he wring it out, as was explained in siman 320)
- **séif 29, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « מפני שאינן לרפואה אלא כדי שלא יסרטו בגדיו את המכה | במה דברים אמורים כשלא היו מעולם על המכה… (28 mots) » → because they are not for refuah, but only so that his garments should not scratch the wound … / In what case are these words said? When they were never on the wound; but if they were already on the wound — they no longer heal, even though they are old, and it is permitted to place them upon a wound on Shabbos
- **séif 30, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu ; abregee ici, texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 30) — « שאינו אלא כמשמרה | והוא הדין כל עלין שהן מרפאין ואם נתנם עליה מבעוד יום… (40 mots) | אפלו עודה בידו | ויחליק הגומות שבה ויתחיב משום ממחק… אבל אם (au lieu de « ואם ») | משחשכה | ואפלו אם נפלה ממנו לגמרי… (122 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → for it only protects it … and the same applies to all leaves that heal. And if he placed them on it while it was still day and removed them from it after dark, even deliberately — it is permitted to replace it, because they forbade any refuah on account of the decree of grinding ingredients only when he begins it on Shabbos … / …even while it is still in his hand … and smooth out its hollows and become liable for memachek (smoothing), as was explained in siman 314. / But if it slipped off the wound by itself after dark … And even if it fell off him entirely — its law is like one that slipped. / In what case are these words said? When it fell onto a utensil; but if it fell onto the ground … it is forbidden … / And through a non-Jew it is permitted to place it on the wound even for the first time … But it is forbidden to tell a non-Jew to make the bandage on Shabbos … / A person may uncover part of the bandage and wipe the mouth of the wound and return it onto it … But he should not wipe the bandage, because he is smearing
- **séif 31, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שלא יסרטוה בגדיו ואין לחוש שמא ימרח או ישחוק סמנים שכיון שכבר נתרפאה אינו בהול עליה כל כך… » → so that his garments should not scratch it. And there is no concern lest he smear or grind ingredients, for since it has already healed — he is not so anxious about it that he would come to smearing and grinding ingredients
- **séif 32, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu ; abregee ici, texte complet dans le sa-en du séif 32) — « אם… בשביל שיכנס בה האויר לרפואה | מפני שהוא מתקן לה פתח וכל המתקן פתח… (66 mots) | ולא כדי להכניס בו אויר לרפואה | ואף על פי שממילא נעשה פתח… (114 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → if it is in order to make for it an opening so that air may enter it for refuah … because he fashions an opening for it; and anyone who fashions an opening for any detached thing — is liable on account of repairing a utensil (tikkun keli), which is a derivative of "makeh be-patish," as was explained in siman 314; and what difference is there between repairing a utensil and repairing a wound? … this opening too is made for bringing in air and taking out pus continually. / …and not in order to bring air into it for refuah — it is permitted. And even though an opening fit for bringing in and taking out is formed of itself … this is a melachah she-einah tzerichah legufah. / And even according to those who say that one is liable for a melachah she-einah tzerichah legufah … and because of his pain they did not decree. / And there is one who says that they permitted it only when he lances it for the need of the present pus alone … it is forbidden. And it is good to be concerned for his words, to do it through a non-Jew
- **séif 33, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « ואינו דומה להוצאת לחה שהלחה אינה מחברת ומבלעת בבשר… (32 mots) » → And it is not comparable to expelling pus, for the pus is not attached and absorbed in the flesh, but is as if deposited and placed within a vessel, and when he lances the boil to expel it — he is merely like one who opens a vessel to take out what is inside it; which is not so with blood, which is attached and absorbed in the flesh
- **séif 34, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אפלו כל שהוא | אבל אם חזר ונסתם יש להסתפק… (71 mots) | ד״פסיק רישה״ הוא אבל מתר להחליף בגד אחר… במה דברים אמורים… (64 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → even by any amount … But if it closed up again — there is room for doubt whether it is permitted to open it again on Shabbos as at first, just as it is permitted to open a hole that became blocked in a utensil, as was explained in siman 314. And likewise those who have a hole in the arm, which is called aptura … And it is permitted to place a bandage on the aptura … And there is one who forbids placing [it] on the aptura (and one should be concerned for his words). / …for it is a "pesik reishei." But it is permitted to change to another cloth, for if he does not change it, it will become foul-smelling, and great is human dignity (kevod ha-beriyos), and he also suffers from it. / In what case are these words said? With this wound that has a hole; but with a wound that has no hole — it is forbidden to change the cloth or the paper … on account of mefarek, as will be explained. And all the more so he should not place tzukker zelb on a wound that has no hole
- **séif 35, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שלא מחמת ברזל | כדי להעמיד הדם | מפני שהוא חזק ויש בו משום רפואה | מועיל | ואסור כשאין המכה על גב היד או על גב הרגל… (26 mots) » → not by iron … in order to stop the blood … because it is strong and involves refuah. And if he is delicate (me'unag) — even the wine is effective for him like the vinegar, and it is forbidden — when the wound is not on the back of the hand or the back of the foot, for then it is permitted to desecrate for it even with a full melachah; and likewise if it is from a blow of iron, as was explained above
- **séif 36, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « דהינו שיצא העצם ממקומו | שזהו רפואתו | ואם נתרפא נתרפא » → namely the bone came out of its place … for that is its remedy … and if he is healed — he is healed
- **séif 37, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שהם כמין רצועות דקות… | כיון שהם קרובים להנתק | אפלו כשחותכן בכלי אלא שאסור מדברי סופרים | שאין זו דרך גזיזה | והוא שפרשו כלפי מעלה… (58 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → which are like thin strips that have detached from the skin of the finger around the nail … since they are close to being severed … even when he cuts them with an instrument, but it is forbidden by the words of the Sages. / …which is not the manner of shearing … This is provided that they detached upward, namely that they began to detach toward the nail. And some say that "upward" means toward the body and not toward the nail. And one must be concerned for both interpretations. / And if most of them did not detach and he took them off by hand — he is exempt, but it is forbidden. If he cut them with an instrument — he is liable on account of gozez; and even according to those who say that one is exempt for a melachah she-einah tzerichah legufah — some say that with gozez one is liable according to all, for the reason that will be explained in siman 340
- **séif 38, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « שנכר הוא שהוא לרפואה | או מטבל בו פתו ויאכלנו כדרכו בחל ואפלו על ידי נכרי אסור… (64 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → for it is recognizable that it is for refuah … or dip his bread in it and eat it in his usual weekday manner. / And even through a non-Jew it is forbidden to do anything for him, even though that thing has no trace of melachah, not even of the words of the Sages, but only that it is recognizable that it is for refuah. / In what case are these words said? With a mere ailment; but if he is in such pain that his whole body is weakened by it — it is permitted to do through a non-Jew anything whose prohibition is only of the words of the Sages, as was explained above
- **séif 39, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « דהינו להשהות שמן בפיו טרם שיבלענו | שנכר שמכון לרפואה | ואם נתרפא נתרפא ויש אוסרים אפלו לבלוע… (60 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → namely keep the oil in his mouth before he swallows it, for it is recognizable that he intends refuah … and if he is healed — he is healed. / And some forbid even swallowing, because oil is harmful to the body, and it is not customary to drink it except for refuah. And everything follows the place and the time: if it is not the custom of the healthy to swallow it — it is forbidden. / But it is permitted to put much oil into anigron and swallow it, for then it does not harm the body … Provided only that he swallows it immediately and does not keep it in his mouth; and needless to say that he should not spit it out, for then it is recognizable that he intends refuah
- **séif 40, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « מפני שמפרק כלאחר יד הוא שאין דרך לינק בפיו… (65 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → because it is mefarek ki-le'achar yad (extracting in an unusual manner), for it is not the way to suckle with one's mouth, but to milk into a vessel and drink from it; therefore suckling involves no Torah prohibition but only one of the words of the Sages, and because of his pain they did not decree. / In what case are these words said? With the pain of one who groans; but if he has only the pain of hunger — it is forbidden to suckle from the animal on Shabbos; but on Yom Tov it is permitted to suckle (if it is impossible for him to milk through a non-Jew, and he also has no food to milk into)
- **séif 41, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu (la page FR omet le renvoi au siman 320, rendu ici depuis l'hebreu)) — « או לתוך הקדרה להניק את בנה | מפני שהחולב לתוך כלי מפרק גמור הוא… | שכיון שהחלב הזה הולך לאבוד… (59 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → or into a pot to nurse her son, because one who milks into a vessel is a full mefarek and is liable on account of dash (threshing), and they permitted a full melachah only where there is danger. / …for since this milk goes to waste — there is in it no Torah-level mefarek, but only one of the words of the Sages, as was explained in siman 320, and for the need of the infant they did not decree. / But it is forbidden to spray some of her milk on one upon whom an evil spirit (ru'ach ra'ah) has blown, because there is in it no danger, nor great pain such that we would permit him a prohibition of the words of the Sages through a Jew, as they permitted for one who groans and the like; see the end of siman 330
- **séif 42, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « מין שרף הנקרא | אימתי | אם אינו מתכון אלא » → the kind of resin called matzteki … When? When he intends refuah; but if he intends it only on account of mouth-odor — it is permitted
- **séif 43, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « לרפואה אף על פי שהם קשים לקצת דברים… (45 mots) | גזרה משום שחיקת סמנים | והוא שיש לו מחוש בעלמא… (74 mots, jusqu'à la fin) » → even though they are hard on some things and it is somewhat recognizable that he intends refuah; such as spleen … or vetch (karshinin) … it is permitted to eat them also for refuah. / …by decree on account of grinding ingredients. This is when he has a mere ailment and is strengthening himself and walking about like a healthy person, even though he is in great pain. But if his whole body has become sick from it … it was already explained above that there are those who permit. / But if he has no ailment at all … such as one who eats sweet resins and swallows a raw egg in order to make the voice pleasant — it is permitted. But when he intends refuah — it is forbidden, even though he is entirely healthy and has no ailment whatsoever
- **séif 44, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « דהינו גרמת קיא | שבמעיו שמתוך כך הוא רעב וחוזר ואוכל | מפני שדומה לרפואה | עד שיקיא » → namely inducing vomiting … because of the waste of the food in his innards, for as a result he becomes hungry and eats again … because it resembles refuah … until he vomits
- **séif 45, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אף על פי שעדין יש בו הבל | דהינו גידי האזן שיורדין לפעמים ומתפרקין הלחיים מעלין אותם בין ביד בין בכלי וכן להעלות (au lieu de « ולהעלות ») | דהינו תנוך שכנגד הלב שנכפף לצד פנים מפני שכל אחד מאלו… » → even though there is still warmth in it … namely the tendons of the ear that sometimes descend so that the jaws become dislocated — one raises them, whether by hand or with a utensil. And likewise to raise the unkali, namely the cartilage opposite the heart that has bent inward. For each of these is not done with drugs, such that we would be concerned about grinding, and he suffers from them
- **séif 46, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « מטעם זה ואין ללמוד מזה התר לאותם שנוהגים… (32 mots) » → for this reason. And one should not derive from this a permission for those who are accustomed in some lands to put into the nose ash of a crushed herb to dispel the drunkenness, for that ash acts as a refuah for other things as well, and the decree of grinding ingredients applies to it
- **séif 47, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אפלו לתענוג בעלמא כמו שנתבאר בסימן שכ״ז | וכן אסור ליגע עצמו בשאר דבר כדי שיזיע לרפואה גזרה שמא ישחק וישתה סמנים המביאים זעה | שמא יבא להשקותו סמנים המשלשלים » → even for mere pleasure, as was explained in siman 327 … And likewise it is forbidden to exert oneself by any other means in order to sweat for refuah, by decree lest he grind and drink drugs that bring on sweat. / …lest one come to give him laxative drugs to drink
- **séif 48, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « אף על פי שהם מלוחים | לפי שכן דרך לרחוץ בהם אף שלא לרפואה ואינו נכר שמכון לרפואה | שהם מאוסים ואין דרך לרחוץ בהם שלא לרפואה במה דברים אמורים כששוהה בהם אבל אם (au lieu de « ואם ») | שאינו נראה אלא כמקר ובמקום שאין דרך… » → even though they are salty, because it is customary to bathe in them even not for refuah, and it is not recognizable that he intends refuah. / …for they are repulsive, and it is not customary to bathe in them not for refuah. / In what case are these words said? When he tarries in them; but if he does not tarry — it is permitted, for he appears only as one cooling himself. / And in a place where it is not customary to bathe in the hot springs of Tiveria except for refuah — it is forbidden to bathe in them on Shabbos for refuah, even if he does not tarry in them
- **séif 49, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « ולא בטיט שטובעין בו | אפלו הוא מאכל בריאים ואינו שותהו לרפואה כלל | וכתוב וקראת לשבת ענג » → nor in mud in which one sinks … even if it is food of the healthy and he does not drink it for refuah at all … and it is written: "ve-karasa la-Shabbos oneg" ("and you shall call the Shabbos a delight")
- **séif 50, EN** (portee depuis la page FR) — « אף על פי שאינן רצין אחריו | אפלו להמחיבים במלאכה שאינה צריכה לגופה » → even though they are not running after him … even according to those who hold one liable for a melachah she-einah tzerichah legufah
- **séif 51, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « והוא הדין מה שעושין לאדם שחש בעיניו שמקיפין אותו בטבעת כדי שלא יתפשט הנפח » → And the same applies to what is done for a person whose eyes ail, that they encircle it with a ring so that the swelling should not spread
- **séif 52, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « מפני שדומה לבונה (ועוד שכל רפואה אסורה) ואפלו לשפשפו הרבה בצונן אסור כמו שנתבאר למעלה | ובמקום סכנת אבר לא גזרו » → because it resembles building (boneh) (and moreover, because all refuah is forbidden). And even to rub it much in cold water — is forbidden, as was explained above. / …and where there is danger to a limb (sakanas ever) they did not decree
- **séif 53, EN** (portee depuis la page FR, controlee sur l'hebreu) — « ואף על פי שמקלקל הוא… ואי אפשר לו להוציא תחלה את הדם… | וכל החבורה ואחר כך כורך עליה סמרטוט ויש מפקפקים… (fin du séif) » → And even though he is spoiling it (mekalkel), nonetheless it is forbidden by the words of the Sages; and all the more so with a red cloth, which he improves.
